Last updated: 2026-09-10 (**An emptied bin stays emptied - conversations from other channels no longer come straight back on the next check** - `unified-inbox` only, uncommitted working tree. NEW migration `053_provider_deletions.sql`. **THE BUG:** delete a live chat, an enquiry, a call or a text, empty the bin, and the next collection copied the lot back in - confirmed on the live Deskwell database, where 24 conversations dating back to 9 August were re-created at 21:12 on 2026-09-10. Email has been safe since 001 because `uin_processed_messages` keeps its `(connection, folder, uid)` row with a NULL `thread_id`; the channels had no equivalent, and the owning module is the source of truth and offers the conversation again on every pass. **THE AMPLIFIER:** `providerWatermarks()` derived `since` from `MAX(last_message_at)` over SURVIVING provider threads, so emptying a bin wound the watermark BACKWARDS and the next pass re-listed weeks of settled ground - which is why one deletion returned a month of chats. **NEW TABLE `uin_provider_deleted`** (`provider_module` TEXT, `external_id` TEXT, `through` TIMESTAMP(3), `deleted_at` TIMESTAMP(3), PK on the first two) - a gravestone holding the channel's own id and the newest message the conversation held, and deliberately no name, address or body, so it outlives a D17 erasure without keeping what was erased. **`deleteThreads()` writes it** (in `lib/db.ts`, so all three destroy paths get it: Empty bin, the retention sweep and person erasure), covering merged-in losers too since the external id stays on the losing side. **NEW `providerDeletionFloors(providerModule, externalIds)`** in `lib/db.ts`; **`providerWatermarks()` now UNIONs the gravestones** so the watermark only moves forwards. **`syncProvider()` reads the floors once per pass** and skips a conversation whose `lastMessageAt <= through` - not opened, not upserted, not counted - and where the party HAS written since, files only messages with `sentAt > through`, so a chat that reopens carries the new words and not the destroyed history. Letting it back at all is deliberate: the far end never closed the conversation, so a customer typing into a chat the site had silently stopped reading is the worse and more invisible failure. **TESTS:** three new cases in `lib/provider-sync.test.ts` (25 pass) and claim 8 in `lib/bin.live.test.ts`, which is real SQL on a throwaway VPS database - `npm run test:inbox-bin`, 7 pass. No permission key, route, env var or Puck block changed.)

Last updated: 2026-09-10 (**Suggest reply - a generic reply-drafting seam in core, google-ai-studio publishing it, unified-inbox and contact-form consuming it** - core plus `google-ai-studio`, `unified-inbox`, `contact-form` and one line of `contact-form-reply-catcher`, all uncommitted. **WHAT IT IS:** a **Suggest reply** button under the writing box wherever this site has one. It sends the conversation off, gets three drafts back, and clicking one puts it in the box to read; a tick keeps it and a cross puts back whatever was written before. **THE SEAM IS THE POINT.** NEW extension point **`core.reply-suggestions`** (`serverOnly`): the module holding the conversation never learns which model answered, and the module that answered never learns where the words came from. Core names neither end. **NEW `lib/conversations/types.ts` additions** - `REPLY_SUGGESTER_POINT`, `ReplySuggestionMessage`, `ReplySuggestionRequest`, `ReplySuggester`, `ResolvedReplySuggester`. A message carries a **`role` of `them`/`us`/`note`, never a direction**: a note is colleagues talking among themselves, which is context worth having and must never be quoted back at a customer, and `in`/`out` cannot say that. **NEW `lib/conversations/reply-suggestion-error.ts`** - `ReplySuggestionError` and `isReplySuggestionError`, in a file of its own **with no imports at all**, because both ends need it: `types.ts` is imported for its types from both halves of the app and must stay free of runtime code, and a module publishing a suggester cannot import the resolver (the registry imports that module). `isReplySuggestionError` matches by name+shape as well as `instanceof`, since one class can be evaluated twice across a bundle boundary and a perfectly good explanation would otherwise arrive as a shrug. **NEW `lib/conversations/reply-suggestions.ts`** - shaped exactly like `providers.ts`/`destinations.ts` (`INSTALLED_MODULE_WHERE`, shape check, shrug at an entry the bundle has not caught up with, **dynamic `await import('@/lib/modules/extension-points')`** to keep out of a registry cycle). Exports `REPLY_SUGGESTION_COUNT` (3), `resolveReplySuggesters`, `firstConfiguredReplySuggester`, `canSuggestReplies()`, `trimTranscript(messages, 24000)` and `suggestReplies()`. `canSuggestReplies` is asked while a reply box is being DRAWN, so it returns before touching the database on a site with nothing published, and `isConfigured()` is cheap by contract. **NEW `components/admin/ReplySuggestions.tsx`** - core chrome, because a reply box is not a rare thing. It **owns no writing box**: `onPreview(text | null)` and `onAccept(text)` are the whole contract, so a contentEditable host, a markdown host and a plain textarea host each keep their own. The host stashes what was there on the **FIRST** preview only - flicking between three drafts and then crossing out must restore what somebody actually wrote, not the draft they looked at before this one. **GOOGLE-AI-STUDIO:** NEW `migrations/003_reply_suggestions.sql` (idempotent `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` for `text_model` and `reply_house_style` on the `gas_settings` singleton; two strings read with the six already there did not earn a table). NEW `lib/gemini-text.ts` - `POST .../v1beta/models/{model}:generateContent`, 30s timeout, `responseMimeType: application/json` plus a `responseSchema` of ARRAY-of-STRING with min/max = count. **The schema is load-bearing:** a model asked politely for three numbered replies will one day send two, or four, or a preamble. NEW `lib/reply-prompt.ts` (pure, tested) - house style, then the transcript between fences, then the instruction; **it says the transcript is DATA before the fences as well as after**, and `defuse()` takes any closing fence out of a message, because a stranger typed those words. NEW `lib/reply-suggestions.ts` - the `ReplySuggester` itself, carrying `GoogleAiError`'s own wording and status across into `ReplySuggestionError` so "check the key" and a 429 still read as themselves. Settings, its route and its tab gain both fields; the tab now has **Pictures** and **Replies** headings. `requiresCoreVersion` -> **0.5.1574** on all three modules. **UNIFIED-INBOX:** NEW `lib/reply-transcript.ts` - its own query rather than `listThreadMessages`, because the screen never receives a message's markup (it is fetched into a sandboxed frame one at a time) and anything READING the conversation needs the words inside it; newest 40 out of the database, reversed, `body_text` falling back to `htmlToText(readableHtml(body_html))`. NEW `app/api/threads/[id]/suggest-reply/route.ts` (`unifiedinbox.reply` **and** `canOpenThread` - a draft about a conversation somebody may not read would be a way of reading it). `Composer.tsx` gains `canSuggestReplies`, `offerSuggestions` (reply and reply-all only), `previewSuggestion`/`acceptSuggestion` and an effect that **clears the stash when the panel goes** - a preview left in the box keeps its words, but a stale stash would put a five-minute-old draft back on the next cross. Preview marks nothing dirty; accept does everything a keystroke does. Threaded through `ThreadPane` and `ComposerSlot`; `InboxPanel` resolves it in the **second** `Promise.all` (`canReplyHere.then(...)`) - the first one runs before `canReply` exists and putting it there is a TDZ crash. **CONTACT-FORM:** NEW `lib/thread.ts` - `threadMessagesFor` (the `contact-form.thread-messages` merge lifted out of the detail page, permission-filtered exactly as it was) and `transcriptFor`; the page now calls it, so what it DRAWS and what a suggestion READS cannot disagree. NEW `app/api/admin/contact-form/submissions/[id]/suggest-reply/route.ts` (`contact.reply`). `ThreadMessageContribution` gains an optional **`direction`** - the point carried both ways all along (reply-catcher contributes admin-sent mail as well as submitter replies) and nothing could tell them apart; absent means `in`, which is what the point meant before. `contact-form-reply-catcher` sets it, one line, **optional type seam so either order ships**. **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `npm test` green (6773 passed, 0 failed, 429 skipped; one earlier run had `lib/puck/module-layout-site-logo.test.tsx` time out under parallel load and it passes on its own at a longer timeout - the AdminNav/sidebar-order changes in this shared tree are another session's), `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a **real PASS 4/4** with `google-ai-studio` among the schemas built - owed because a module migration adds columns. NEW tests: `modules/google-ai-studio/lib/reply-prompt.test.ts` (9) and `lib/conversations/reply-suggestions.test.ts` (4). **NOT verified in a browser** - visual checking is Chris's.)

Last updated: 2026-09-10 (**Sidebar links can be dragged into any order, favourites included** - core, uncommitted working tree. No schema change, no migration, no new permission key, no env var, no new dependency. **WHAT IT IS:** a per-user arrangement of the admin sidebar, held in `localStorage` under `cactus-sidebar-item-order` as `Record<sectionId, itemId[]>` - deliberately NOT a site setting. Settings > Navigation already owns the shared, permissioned ordering (`AdminMenuConfig.items[].order`, gated behind `config.manage`), and one person tidying their own rail must not move everybody else's. The server still resolves which sections and items exist and who may see them; this only re-orders what it sent. **NEW `lib/nav/sidebar-order.ts`** - the whole ordering algebra, pure and React-free so it can be tested on its own: `parseNavOrder` (localStorage is external input, so a hand-edited or half-written value yields `{}` rather than a crash on first paint), `applyNavOrder`, `orderAfterDrop`, `orderAfterKeyboardMove`, `favouritesAfterDrop`, `favouritesAfterKeyboardMove`. `applyNavOrder` holds the four invariants worth knowing: an id listed under a section it did not come from renders THERE (cross-section drags are the point); the first section to claim an id wins, so a stale arrangement listing a link twice still renders it once; an id the server no longer sends is dropped; and an id the arrangement has never seen - a link a module added since - keeps its server position at the END of its own section. `orderAfterDrop` writes back EVERY section, not only the two involved, because a whole-tree snapshot is the one shape that cannot half-apply when the menu changes underneath it. **NEW `lib/nav/sidebar-order.test.ts`** - 18 tests, all of the above plus both keyboard ends. **NEW `components/admin/useNavReorder.ts`** - the pointer half: hit testing, auto-scroll, click suppression, focus restoration. **POINTER EVENTS, NOT HTML5 DRAG-AND-DROP, AND THAT IS THE REQUIREMENT:** native dragging swaps the cursor for a move/alias glyph and drags a translucent ghost of the link, and this was asked for explicitly not to change the cursor - so there is NO `cursor` rule anywhere in the feature, `draggable={false}` sits on every nav `<Link>` to kill the browser's own link drag, and the only things that move are the carried row (dimmed to 0.45) and a 2px `--color-primary` insertion line. Under 5px of movement is still a click; past that, `consumeClick()` swallows the click the browser synthesises on pointerup so a drag never navigates. **TWO DRAG ZONES, `tree` and `fav`, and a row never crosses between them** - a link joins or leaves Favourites by its star, and nothing else; the zone rides on `data-drag-zone` and a hit test in the wrong zone returns no target. Filter results are a search list and take no drag props at all. **DROPPING INTO A SECTION WITH NO ROW TO AIM AT:** section headings and emptied sections carry `data-drag-section`, so a link can be moved into a section that is collapsed or that you have just emptied (an expanded empty section draws a dashed `.admin-nav-drop-empty` strip while a tree drag is in flight). Dropping onto a collapsed heading fires `onSectionDrop`, which opens that section - otherwise the link looks exactly like it vanished. **TOUCH IS LEFT ALONE** (`e.pointerType === 'touch'` returns early): the same vertical gesture scrolls the rail, and stealing it would leave the menu unscrollable on a phone. **KEYBOARD:** `Alt+ArrowUp` / `Alt+ArrowDown` on a focused link steps it one place and follows it with focus (`aria-keyshortcuts` announces it); stepping off the end of a section moves it into the next, exactly as a drag does. `onNavKeyDown`'s roving Up/Down now bails on `altKey` so the two do not fight. **RESET:** a toolbar button appears only once `hasCustomOrder`, and clears the key. **`components/admin/AdminNav.tsx`:** favourites persistence lifted into `persistFavourites` (now returns its argument, so `persistSections` does too and both read as one expression); renders `reorder.sections` rather than the `sections` prop; `filterMatches` now built from the ordered items so search results follow the arrangement. **`app/globals.css`:** `.admin-nav--dragging` (user-select only), `.admin-nav-row--dragging`, `.admin-nav-row--drop-before/after`, `.admin-nav-section-label--drop`, `.admin-nav-drop-empty`. The insertion line sits at `top:0`/`bottom:0` rather than `-1px` so the favourites' own overflow scroller cannot clip it. **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `npm test` green (6772 passed, 429 skipped - the single `lib/puck/module-layout-site-logo.test.tsx` timeout under parallel load passes on its own, and the `modules/unified-inbox` type errors seen mid-run are another session's in-flight work in this shared tree). No schema change and no raw SQL, so no backup round-trip or ledger guard was owed. **NOT verified in a browser** - visual checking is Chris's.)

Five tabs: Settings, Spaces & layouts, Model corrections, Sizes, Pictures. The four screens are `next/dynamic` with `ssr:false` and load only when their tab is opened - Sizes alone pulls the 3D stack in to measure models in the browser, and Shop settings is opened far more often to change a delivery charge than to rebuild a catalogue's dimensions. **NEW `lib/admin/use-spl-settings.ts`** - loads the settings and reports `loading | ready | forbidden | failed`. It exists because the tab strip has to be drawn BEFORE anybody clicks Settings, so whether the account may read the settings has to be known up front: the endpoint answers to `space-planner.manage` while the panel now opens on `space-planner.access` (widened from `.manage` in the manifest, so the staff who could see the old sidebar link still see these screens). No schema change and no raw SQL, so no backup round-trip or ledger guard was owed. **NOT verified in a browser** - visual checking is Chris's.)

Last updated: 2026-09-10 (**A rate limit now waits itself out instead of stopping the job** - `google-ai-studio` v0.1.1, same unreleased tree as the entry below. **THE RULE:** a 429 from Google is not a failure - the key is fine, the request is fine, only the moment is wrong - so the browser waits and asks again, indefinitely, until it has a picture or the run is called off. Everything else still fails at once. **WHY THE BROWSER AND NOT THE SERVER:** a module route has the shared 60s dispatcher ceiling and a quota window has considerably more than that, so a server-side retry would simply time out instead. **gemini.ts:** `GoogleAiError` gains `retryAfterSeconds`; NEW exported `parseRetryAfter(header, now)` reads both legal shapes (delta-seconds, HTTP-date), rejects absent/unparseable/past values and caps at 3600 so a stray header cannot park a browser on a countdown until Christmas; `describeHttpFailure` takes it as a third argument and `generateImage` passes `response.headers.get('retry-after')`. The 429's own sentence lost its "Wait a minute and try again" - nobody is being asked to do that any more. **THE ROUTE:** `jobs/[id]/images` answers a 429 with `NextResponse.json({error, retryable: true, retryAfterSeconds}, {status: 429})` rather than plain `errorResponse`, because the browser needs a fact to act on, not only a sentence. Every other `GoogleAiError` is unchanged. **NEW `lib/backoff.ts`** - `retryDelaySeconds(attempt, retryAfterSeconds, jitter)`: Google's own header wins outright; otherwise 15s, 25s, 40s, 60s and then 60s forever, plus up to 5s of jitter. **The jitter is load-bearing** - four pictures rate-limited together would otherwise retry on the same clock, arrive together and be refused together indefinitely. `NaN`/0/negative attempts fall back to the first step (`STEPS[index] ?? STEPS[0]`, and the fallback is reachable: `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` is on and `STEPS[NaN]` is undefined). NEW `lib/backoff.test.ts`, 9 tests, covering both it and `parseRetryAfter`. **THE PANEL:** `makeOneImage(id, signal)` returns a **discriminated `ImageResult`** (`{ok:true,candidate}` | `{ok:false,reason:'aborted'}` | `{ok:false,reason:'failed',message}`) rather than throwing - "the owner pressed Stop" is not an error and must not be told apart from one by reading a message, and an aborted `fetch` throws a `TypeError` whose `.message` is "Failed to fetch", which is not English for a site owner. `countDown(seconds, signal, tick)` sleeps in one-second slices purely so the countdown on screen is honest. **ONE `AbortController` per run** in `runRef`, ended by: the **Stop** button, **Discard**, the unmount cleanup (a client-side navigation to another page) and a `pagehide` listener (closing the tab, a hard navigation) - which is exactly the "until we close the tab/move to another page" this was asked for. `stoppedByOwnerRef` decides who gets told: only the Stop button leaves a message, because Discard already said what the owner wanted and a panel that has gone has nobody left to tell. Pictures already made stay on screen to be kept or thrown away - they were paid for either way. **UI:** the primary button reads `Waiting {n}s…`, a **Stop** button appears beside it, and an `alert-info` explains what is happening and that leaving the page open is all that is needed. **TWO UI FIXES IN THE SAME PASS, both reported off the live Deskwell product editor:** (1) the **House style** box was the width of a postcode - a `<textarea>` carries its own intrinsic width from `cols` and only the prompt box escaped it by sitting in a stretching flex column, so `.gas-card textarea` now sets `display:block;width:100%;box-sizing:border-box;resize:vertical` outright rather than relying on where a box happens to sit. (2) The source groups are now `<details>` (`.gas-fold`), **open only for the product's own photographs** - a range with forty variations was forty tiles to scroll past on every visit to reach the prompt. Each folded summary carries its picture count AND how many are ticked inside it, because a fold that hides ticks with no way to see them is worse than the scroll. `display:flex` on a `<summary>` takes the browser's own disclosure triangle with it, so the panel draws its own from borders in `currentColor` (a token) and rotates it on `[open]`, with the transition dropped under `prefers-reduced-motion`. **AND THE TRAP, HIT AGAIN:** a CSS comment written in the usual prose style, naming a property in backticks, TERMINATES the template literal that module CSS lives in - `tsc` reports `TS1005 ',' expected` at a line past the real one. Already in memory for `components/public/*-css.ts`; `components/admin/panel-css.ts` is the same edge and the memory now says so. **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `npm test` green (6742 passed, 429 skipped). No schema change and no new raw SQL in this half, so nothing further was owed.)

Last updated: 2026-09-10 (**Every module's sidebar icon was being thrown away, so every module link drew the puzzle piece** - core, uncommitted working tree. No schema change, no route, no permission, no env var. **THE BUG:** a module supplies its sidebar icon as an SVG *fragment* - a bare run of `<path>`/`<circle>`, no `<svg>` root, because `components/admin/AdminNav.tsx` supplies the root (and with it the viewBox, the stroke width and `currentColor`) and injects the fragment inside it. `app/cactus-admin/layout.tsx` scrubbed each one with `sanitizeSvg`, which hands the markup to DOMPurify with no root element: it parses in HTML context, where `<path>` is an unknown element, and every one came back as `''`. `buildModuleNavGroups` then set `icon: undefined`, `moduleItemFrom` fell to `icon: 'modules'`, and Catalogue, Trading, Purchase Orders, Bookkeeping, SEO, Boards, Gazette, Directory and Space Planner all drew the generic module puzzle piece. Every one of those modules has shipped a proper icon in its manifest since it was written - nothing was ever missing, and nothing anywhere said so, because a stripped icon renders as a valid-looking fallback. **FIX: NEW `sanitizeSvgFragment` in `lib/sanitize.ts`** - wraps the fragment in an `<svg>` root so the parser is in SVG context, sanitises, then peels the root back off; a module that supplies a whole `<svg>` is unwrapped too, so either shape works. Same DOMPurify profile as before, so `<script>` and event handlers still go. `sanitizeSvg` itself is unchanged - `lib/media/upload.ts` passes it whole documents and wants exactly what it does today. **NEW `lib/sanitize-svg-fragment.test.ts`** (4 tests, plain `npm test`, no key): a bare fragment survives, a whole `<svg>` is unwrapped, script/onclick are still stripped, and markup with nothing drawable is still `''`. **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint` clean on the three files, the 4 new tests pass. No SQL. **NOT verified in a browser** - visual checking is Chris's.)

Last updated: 2026-09-10 (**The pick is shared between the list and the open conversation, and its bar is drawings** - `unified-inbox` only, uncommitted working tree. No schema change, no new route, no new permission, no new env var. **NEW `components/admin/inbox/Selection.tsx`:** `SelectionProvider` (mounted in `InboxPanel` directly inside `UndoProvider`, wrapping BOTH columns), `useSelection()` -> `{ selected, setSelected, picked, pickedRows, clear }`, a separate write-only `useRegisterRows(rows)` context the LIST alone calls (an effect, guarded by a field-by-field `same()` so a fresh server render's new array does not redraw the panel), `useBulkTargets(threadId)` -> `[threadId, ...picked]` de-duplicated, plus helpers `them(n)`, `runOnMany(ids, send)` -> `{ failed, count }` (Promise.allSettled, one request per conversation) and `refusalMessage(failed, count)`. `PickedRow` = `{ id, status, snoozeUntil: string | null, unread }` - ISO string, not Date, because putting a snoozed conversation back needs the date the API wants. Outside a provider `useSelection()` returns a frozen empty pick, so any component can call it. **`ThreadListView`:** the `selected` useState is gone, replaced by the context's; `onScreen`/`picked`/`pickedRows` are still derived LOCALLY and synchronously from `rows` (the pane's copy arrives an effect later, which is fine for a button and not fine for a bar); registers `shape` (rows mapped to `PickedRow`). **BULK BAR NOW ICONS:** `Mark as done` -> the word **Done**, `Open again` -> the word **Reopen** (the only two words left - they say where a conversation STANDS, and no glyph reads as "filed"); read/unread/snooze/spam/delete/put-back/merge are `uin-icon-btn uin-icon-btn-framed` buttons inside `AdminTooltip`, each with an `sr-only` sentence naming the count; `Clear` stays a `uin-chip` and gains `.uin-bulk-clear { margin-left: auto }`. **NEW icons** in `inbox/icons.tsx`: `MailOpenIcon`, `MailSealedIcon`, `RestoreIcon`, `MergeIcon`. **`inbox.css`:** `.uin-bulk` gap 0.4 -> 0.3rem and still `flex-wrap: wrap` (a 260px column cannot hold one line); `.uin-bulk > *, .uin-bulk .btn { flex: none; white-space: nowrap }`; `.uin-bulk-count` no longer wraps. **THIRD COLUMN ACTS ON THE PICK:** `BinButton`, `SpamButton` and `ThreadActions` (Snooze, and the status menu's Done/Open) all run over `useBulkTargets(threadId)` - the conversation being read PLUS everything ticked in the list - clear the pick afterwards, and say the count in the tooltip, the `sr-only` text, the spam dialog and the undo toast. `ThreadActions.whereTheyStood()` builds the undo from `pickedRows` for the ticked ones and from its own props for the open one. **Handing it to somebody is deliberately NOT bulk** - `patch(body, { all: false })` is the escape hatch and only the two assignee `MenuItem`s use it. **The block offer stays single**: the spam dialog only ever offers to turn away the sender of the conversation actually being read, and says so when a pile is moving. **THE CHANNEL BLOCK MOVED INTO THE JUNK QUESTION:** `BlockParticipant` is now UNBLOCK-ONLY (props `{ threadId, channelLabel }`, no `blocked`; renders `.uin-thread-blocked` + "Unblock them") and `ThreadPane` draws it only when `blockState?.blocked`. The red "Block them" button is gone from the thread header. `SpamButton` gains `channelBlock: { label, blocked } | null` (passed down `ThreadPane` -> `ThreadActions` -> `SpamButton` off the existing `blockState`); `worthAsking` is now `shutTheList || shutTheChannel` where `shutTheList` = the old address rule and `shutTheChannel` = `canBlock && channelBlock && !channelBlock.blocked && !shutTheList`. `moveAndBlock()` POSTs `threads/[id]/block { blocked: true }` for the channel door and `blocked-senders` for the address one, and the undo toast's second sentence is now a string (`They will not get through on <label> again.` / `The sender stays blocked.`) rather than a boolean. Neither door is ever bulk: only the conversation being read. No route, no permission and no adapter changed - `app/api/threads/[id]/block/route.ts` is untouched. **RAIL CHANNELS WEAR AN ICON, NOT A DOT:** NEW `channelGlyph(key, label)` in `lib/list.ts` -> `'phone' | 'mobile' | 'sms' | 'chat' | 'form' | 'mail' | null`, matched (longest word first) against `` `${label} ${key}`.toLowerCase() `` - never a table of module ids, so a channel published by any module gets the right picture off its own name; null keeps the `toneFor()` coloured dot. `CHANNEL_ICONS` in `NavRail.tsx` maps the glyph names to the JSX (the split exists because `lib/list.ts` is read by server and browser and must not import JSX). `channelEntries` now sets `icon` OR `tone`, never both. NEW `SmartphoneIcon` in `inbox/icons.tsx` (deliberately not a brand mark). Six cases in `lib/list.test.ts`. Wiki updated (`Unified-Inbox.md`, `Twilio.md`), uncommitted. Also fixed: `.uin-rail-more`'s `font: inherit` was wiping the `font-size` `.uin-rail-item` had set, so the rail's More/Less row was drawn at the pane's own size - it now restates `font-size: 0.8125rem` and `line-height: 1.4`.)

Last updated: 2026-09-10 (**Reference photographs made from a product's own 3D model** - `google-ai-studio` **v0.1.1** and `product-3d-views-for-shop` **v0.1.100**, both unreleased and uncommitted. **WHAT IT IS:** a **From 3D models** button in the AI photo section of a product's Images tab. It opens the product's 3D model with one dropdown per variation option; **Create view** takes a still of whatever is on the stage and adds it to the pictures going to Google, so turning the model and pressing again builds up as many angles as the job wants. Only present when both modules are installed. **THE SEAM (the whole design point):** neither module imports, or `requiresModules`, the other, and either may be released first. NEW extension point **`google-ai-studio.reference-image-sources`**, hosted by google-ai-studio and resolved SERVER-side in `components/admin/ProductPhotosSection.tsx` (installed modules from `prisma.module`, components from `moduleExtensionPointComponents`); the resolved nodes are handed down to the client panel as a `sources: {id,label,node}[]` prop and mounted only when the button is pressed - a WebGL context is not a fair price for opening the Images tab. Pictures cross back on a **cancelable `cactus-ai-reference-image` window CustomEvent** carrying `{dataUrl, label}` - the identical shape and reasoning as shop's `cactus-shop-product-gallery-add`: cancelling is how the producer learns anything was listening, and a producer with nobody listening says "update the other module" rather than appearing to do nothing. **THE IMPORT CYCLE THAT BIT:** google-ai-studio already contributes a component to shop's point, so the generated `lib/modules/extension-points.ts` imports `ProductPhotosSection`; a static import back the other way is a registry<->module cycle and `lib/modules/import-cycles.test.ts` fails the build over it. Fix is the one the test prescribes - `await import('@/lib/modules/extension-points')` INSIDE the async server component. **p3d SIDE:** NEW `lib/use-preview-model.ts`, a client hook holding the option-choice -> variation -> model -> `/fabric/[child]` resolve -> `{item, fabric, bundle, pending}` chain that `Product3dEditor`'s brightness preview already had; that panel is refactored onto it (identical behaviour, `bundle` exposed so its "no model of its own" line reads the same). NEW `components/admin/Model3dCapturePanel.tsx` - dropdowns, the viewer, **Create view**, flattens the capture onto WHITE and downsizes to 1280px longest side as `image/jpeg` q0.92 (the canvas is `alpha:true`, so a raw capture is a see-through PNG, which tells the model nothing about the background, and the PNG of a lit 3D model runs to megabytes). `components/public/Viewer3d.tsx` gains ONE optional prop, `captureRef` (`Viewer3dCapture` = `{current: (() => string|null) | null}`): it must reach the renderer/scene/camera that live only in `build()`'s closure, it renders on demand and reads `toDataURL` back SYNCHRONOUSLY (the drawing buffer is cleared at the next composite), and a viewer given one takes `preserveDrawingBuffer` - which joins `generation` and `antialias` in `rendererKey`, since a context's attributes are fixed at creation. The storefront passes no ref and pays nothing. **gas SIDE:** NEW `migrations/002_job_source_images.sql` -> **`gas_job_source_images`** (`job_id` FK CASCADE, `mime_type`, `bytes` BYTEA, `size_bytes`, `label`, `position`), added to `teardown` child-first. Captured views need a home because **every picture a job makes re-reads its references** and a 3D still has no url to re-fetch; they die with the job's 24h sweep. `lib/jobs.ts` gains `addJobSourceImages` (one multi-row INSERT via `Prisma.join`, `decode($n,'base64')`) and `listJobSourceImages` (`encode(bytes,'base64')` - a raw bytea read is a Uint8Array, not always a Buffer). `lib/references.ts` gains `checkInlineReference` and `loadReferences(urls, inline)`; captures go LAST, because Google reads references in order and the last carries most weight. The jobs route takes `captures: [{mimeType, data, label}]`, caps `urls.length + captures.length` at `MAX_REFERENCES` (10) and validates BEFORE the job row exists. **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `npm test` green (6723 passed, 429 skipped) incl. `check-client-graph` and `check-import-cycles`, `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a real PASS 4/4 (owed - a module migration adds a table) with `google-ai-studio` among the schemas built. **The new raw SQL was also EXECUTED** against a throwaway `cactus_rt_*` database on the OVH VPS (migrations applied, `Prisma.join` multi-row insert, bytea round-trip, `size_bytes`, FK cascade) - no standard gate ever runs a query. **NOT verified in a browser** - visual checking is Chris's.)

Last updated: 2026-09-10 (**Nothing about a customer or an order opens on a URL alone any more** - `shop` v0.1.412 (core pin v0.5.1572), and the second half of the receipt work below. No schema change, no new table, no new config. **THE SWEEP:** every public route and page in `modules/shop` plus every module that touches order data was read for "what does this answer to". Clean already: `orders/[id]/live-delivery` and reviews' `order-reviews` (both call `resolveOrderViewer`), the guest order page and its receipt, `track-order/[orderNumber]` (the tracking token names the order and asks for the postcode - it is never a way in), abandoned-carts' `recover` (hands back BASKET LINES only, deliberately never an address), twilio's `audio/[mediaId]` (greeting audio, gated on being referenced by a forwarding rule), product-downloads' `files/[id]`, space-planner's `shared/[token]` (a room layout, nobody's name), `cart/store` and `checkout/session` (POSTs that echo what was sent). **TWO HOLES, BOTH CLOSED. (1) `?email=` IN A URL:** `GET /orders/status?orderNumber=X&email=Y` was still a URL that opened an order - address in the query string, therefore in access logs, browser history and the Referer sent to every third party the page loads, and forwardable besides. That parameter is GONE from `orders/status` and from `orders/notifications`; `getOrderByNumberAndEmail` has no callers left. The old lookup is now a POST: `orders/receipt-access` takes `{orderNumber, answer}` with NO token (the email is checked against the order, one wording for every failure, same per-ORDER lockout) and writes the receipt cookie, after which the status route answers on the cookie with nothing in the address at all. `components/public/OrderLookupClient.tsx` does that in two steps; `OrderConfirmationClient` recognises an old `?email=` link and draws the email gate instead of honouring it. `orders/status` now takes `orderNumber` alone plus an OPTIONAL `t`, and answers 403-with-a-challenge only to a genuine link - a caller without one gets the same 404 whether the order is real or invented. `orders/notifications` gates on `mayOpenReceipt` outright and no longer reads a token at all. **(2) INVOICES, CREDIT NOTES AND PROFORMAS:** all three opened for anyone holding their permanent signed link, forever, and each one carries a name, a billing address, every line and the total. Chris chose to close them. NEW `lib/document-print-token.ts` - a SHORT-LIVED (30 min) HMAC per `{kind, number}`, the purchase-orders pattern, because the PDF printer fetches the document's own page over HTTP with no session and something has to open the door for it; without it, `&print=1` on any permanent link was the whole document. NEW `lib/document-access.ts` - `resolveDocumentAccess` (print token, or staff session, or member-owner, or the postcode-proved `cactus_shop_order_access` cookie), `documentOrder(kind, number)` (a proforma is numbered by its ORDER), `verifyDocumentLinkToken`. NEW `POST /api/m/shop/public/documents/access` - `{kind, number, token, answer}`, same per-order lockout, grants both cookies. NEW `components/public/DocumentAccessGate.tsx` and a fourth mode (`document`) on `OrderAccessForm`, which now refreshes rather than navigating. The three pages draw the gate in place; the three PDF routes redirect to the page (a PDF cannot draw a form). **`allowReceiptCookie` is true on the PDF routes and false on the pages, deliberately:** a rendered page gets a shared-cache header from `proxy.ts` unless the request carries a declared bypass cookie, and `cactus_shop_receipt` is not one - so a page rendered on its strength could be cached under a URL everybody shares. A module API route is never touched by that cache, which is what lets the proforma link on a bank-transfer confirmation still work without asking. **KNOWN INTERACTION:** with the page cache switched ON (Settings > General > Speed, default off), an anonymous GATE render could be cached for that document URL and then shown to somebody who does hold the cookie. Annoying, never a leak, and exactly the same shape as the existing gate on `/shop/account/orders/[id]`. A path-level "never share this" declaration in core would fix both; not built. **LEFT ALONE ON PURPOSE (bearer links to people who cannot ever log in):** quote codes (8 characters of `randomInt` from a 25-symbol alphabet, read down the telephone), the purchase-orders supplier portal (hashed, revocable, scoped to one order), and digital download tokens (the goods themselves). Also left: the account-prompt `registerUrl` on the confirmation still carries the buyer's own email as a query parameter, but it is their address on their own page and only renders as a fallback for an older cached bundle. **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `npm test` green (6713 passed, 429 skipped) plus NEW `lib/document-print-token.test.ts` (10). No schema change and no raw SQL added, so no backup round-trip or ledger guard was owed. **NOT verified in a browser** - visual checking is Chris's.)

Last updated: 2026-09-10 (**A confirmation link no longer opens somebody's receipt on any machine that has it** - `shop` v0.1.412 (core pin v0.5.1572). No schema change, no new table, no new config. **THE HOLE:** `/shop/checkout/confirmation?orderNumber=DW000188&t=<token>` was openable by anyone holding the address - the token (`lib/order-receipt-token.ts`) says WHICH order and nothing about who is asking - and what it shows is the customer's name, their full delivery address, every line they bought and what they paid. Receipt links are forwarded, pasted into group chats, left in shared browsers and synced across households. **THE RULE NOW:** the token still says which order; a second thing has to say the browser may see it. NEW `lib/receipt-access-cookie.ts` - signed httpOnly cookie **`cactus_shop_receipt`** holding up to 10 ORDER NUMBERS, 30 days, granted at the till by `app/api/public/checkout/payment-intent/route.ts` (both branches - the created order and the draft that becomes one - keyed on the order NUMBER, which is minted before either). Deliberately NOT `cactus_shop_order_access`: that one is declared in `cacheBypassCookies`, so granting it at every checkout would take every customer who has ever bought anything out of the shared page cache for a month; this one is read only by the receipt routes, which are not cached (no `publicCacheTtl`), so it costs the cache nothing - and it opens the receipt only, never the order page. NEW `lib/signed-list-cookie.ts` holds the wire format both cookies share (`mintSignedListCookie`/`readSignedListCookie`/`withNewestFirst`, base64url `{o,e}` payload + HMAC namespaced by a purpose string); `lib/guest-order-access.ts` is refactored onto it with its purpose string unchanged (`order-access`), so grants already in customers' browsers keep working. The namespacing is what stops one cookie verifying as the other - asserted in the new tests. **THE CHALLENGE:** NEW `lib/order-receipt-challenge.ts` (`receiptChallengeFor`, `receiptAnswerMatches`) - the delivery postcode, compared through shop's own `normalisePostcode`/`postcodeMatches`, or the customer's email address on an order whose delivery address carries no postcode (a shop delivering somewhere that does not use them; a blank box must never be the key). NEW public route **`POST /api/m/shop/public/orders/receipt-access`** `{orderNumber, token, answer}` - token first (so it cannot be used to ask questions about order numbers), then the same per-ORDER lockout the tracker uses (`lib/db/order-access.ts`, `shp_order_access_attempts`: eight wrong answers locks the order for an hour) plus its own in-memory IP limiter. Passing it grants BOTH cookies, because the answer is the one the tracker would have accepted. **THE GATE:** NEW `mayOpenReceipt(request, order)` and `resolveOrderViewerFromRequest` in `lib/order-viewer.ts` - member-and-owner, or the postcode-proved cookie, or the receipt cookie - asked by `app/api/public/orders/status/route.ts` and `app/api/public/orders/notifications/route.ts` on the TOKEN path only; a failure is **403** carrying `challenge: 'postcode'|'email'`, not a 404. The `?email=` route into both is untouched: somebody typing the address the order was placed with has already answered a question of the same kind. Without the notifications half, a forwarded link could have redirected somebody else's delivery texts to a stranger's phone. **THE PAGE:** `components/public/OrderAccessForm.tsx` gains a third mode, `receipt` - same form, same failure wording, posts to the new route, and calls back rather than navigating; on an email challenge the single box relabels itself. `components/public/OrderConfirmationClient.tsx` renders that gate (new `ReceiptAccessGate`, `TRACK_ORDER_CSS`) on a 403 and re-runs its own fetch once the answer lands; the effect now hangs off an `attempt` counter. No new CSS. **NOT changed:** the guest order page's own postcode gate, the tracker, the `?email=` lookup, the signed proforma link, or what the receipt shows once it is open. **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `npm test` green (6713 passed, 429 skipped) including 2 new suites - `lib/order-receipt-challenge.test.ts` (9) and `lib/receipt-access-cookie.test.ts` (9, one of which is the cross-cookie forgery test). No schema change and no raw SQL added, so no backup round-trip or ledger guard was owed. **NOT verified in a browser** - visual checking is Chris's.)

Last updated: 2026-09-10 (**A new module, `google-ai-studio`, and one small generic seam in shop** - released as **v0.1.0** (commit `a68c2ef`) and pinned in core's `modules.json`. The shop half is NOT released - the seam sits uncommitted in a shop tree another session is working in, and a patch of it is kept in this session's scratchpad. NEW module `modules/google-ai-studio` (prefix `gas_`, `requiresCoreVersion` 0.5.1570, permission `google-ai-studio.manage`, env `ENCRYPTION_KEY` required + `GOOGLE_AI_STUDIO_API_KEY` optional, **no `requiresModules` at all**). Holds one Google AI Studio key and, when a shop is present, hangs an **AI photo creation** panel on the foot of a product's Images tab via `shop.product-editor-media-sections`. **It imports nothing from `@/modules/shop/...`** - it is meant to install on a site with no shop, and that path does not exist at build time on one - so it degrades off TABLE presence (`lib/capabilities.ts`, the `purchase-orders` pattern), reads shop/variations/attributes tables by raw SQL (`lib/sources.ts`), and hands finished pictures to the gallery through a documented window CustomEvent. **SHOP (the only diff outside the new module):** NEW `components/admin/product-editor/gallery-add-bus.ts` (`GALLERY_ADD_EVENT` = `'cactus-shop-product-gallery-add'`, detail `{images: [{url, altText?}]}`, `addImagesToProductGallery()` returning whether it was taken) and a listener in `product-editor/panels/media.tsx` that `preventDefault()`s and appends the images to `state.media` through the existing `commit()` (so a contributed tile after them keeps its slot). Cancelable on purpose: `dispatchEvent` answering true means nothing listened - an older shop - and the sender says the pictures are in the media library instead of pretending. **Shop needs a release for that seam (0.1.412).** **THE API:** Google's Interactions API, `POST https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta/interactions`, `x-goog-api-key`, `store: false`, `response_format {type:'image', aspect_ratio, image_size}`; ONE call makes ONE picture, so the browser makes N of them one at a time (each request stays well inside the 60s module-route ceiling and each picture appears as it lands). Response read from `output_image` OR by walking `steps[].content[]` for a `type:'image'` part; a 200 with no picture is a refusal and is reported as one. **STORAGE:** `gas_settings` (singleton; key AES-GCM via `lib/crypto/secrets`, `image_model` a plain text column so a new Google model needs no module release, `default_image_count` 1-8, `product_photo_prompt`, `aspect_ratio`, `image_size`), `gas_jobs` and `gas_job_images` (`bytes` BYTEA, written `decode($1,'base64')` and read back `encode(bytes,'base64')` - a raw bytea read is a Uint8Array, not always a Buffer). Candidates live there rather than in the media library BECAUSE MOST ARE REJECTED; jobs are swept at 24h from the generate route (no cron). Only an accepted picture is uploaded (`uploadMedia` + `saveMediaRecord`, into the product's own media folder, resolved by the browser from shop's own `media-folder` endpoint) and `gas_job_images.media_id` remembers it so a second press cannot make a second copy. **ROUTES** (all `shop.products` except settings, which is `google-ai-studio.manage`): `admin/settings`, `admin/product-photos/sources`, `admin/product-photos/jobs` (POST), `admin/product-photos/jobs/[id]` (DELETE), `admin/product-photos/jobs/[id]/images` (POST, makes one), `admin/product-photos/candidates/[id]` (GET bytes) and `admin/product-photos/import` (POST). The job route re-lists the product's own sources and refuses any url that is not among them, so the reference fetcher cannot be pointed at anything else. **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `npm test` green (6695 passed, 429 skipped), `check-client-graph` and `check-import-cycles` clean, `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a real PASS (4/4, with `google-ai-studio` among the module schemas built) - owed because a module migration adds tables. **NOT verified in a browser** - visual checking is Chris's.)

Last updated: 2026-09-10 (**A bin, a Delete button, and Spam folded away behind More** - `unified-inbox` **v0.1.71** (core pin v0.5.1572). NEW module migration `migrations/052_bin.sql`: `uin_thread_bin` (`thread_id` TEXT, `user_id` TEXT, `created_at` TIMESTAMP(3), PK on the pair, both FKs `ON DELETE CASCADE`) plus `uin_thread_bin_user_idx (user_id, created_at DESC)` and `uin_thread_bin_thread_idx (thread_id)`. Deliberately a straight copy of `uin_thread_spam`'s shape - deleting is one person's decision about one conversation, not a fact about the mail - with NO `blocked_at` analogue, because nothing ever arrives pre-deleted. Added to `cactus.module.json` `teardown` between `uin_thread_spam` and `uin_thread_inboxes` (`lib/teardown.test.ts` asserts the list and the child-before-parent order). No `migrationCatchups` entry: nothing already released was edited. **NEW `lib/bin.ts`:** `markThreadBinned`, `unmarkThreadBinned`, `threadIsBinnedFor`, and `binOwnerFor` which is `export const binOwnerFor = spamOwnerFor` - the same function object, not a copy, so the two folders can never drift about whose bin something lands in (`lib/bin.test.ts` asserts the identity). Shared address -> the presser's bin; a colleague's own address -> that colleague's bin. **`lib/db.ts`:** `ThreadListFilters` gains `binOnly` and `binOwnerUserId` (undefined = the reader's own, explicit null = a bin belonging to nobody, which yields `false`, exactly as the spam pair does). NEW private `binMatch(viewerUserId)` (reader's own row OR the row of any individual-inbox owner the conversation sits in - `spamMatch` minus the `blocked_at` half) and `binFolderMatch(ownerUserId)`. `filterClauses()` restructured: `binOnly` wins outright and pushes only `binFolderMatch` (so the Bin lists what was deleted, junk included); EVERY other branch - the Spam folder among them - now pushes `NOT binMatch(viewer)` before the junk clause, so a conversation deleted out of the Spam folder leaves it. `openCounts()` and `openAssignedElsewhere()` build their own WHERE and each gained `AND NOT binMatch(viewerUserId)`. NEW exported `binThreadIds({ownerUserId, inboxIds, includeUnrouted, providerModules})` - unpaged, `visibilityClause` AND `binFolderMatch` in one WHERE, which is what the empty route destroys. **`lib/list.ts`:** `InboxParams.binOnly`; `'bin'` added to `SCOPED_FOLDERS` (so `bin:<inbox id>` is a colleague's bin, resolved server-side); `inbox !== 'bin'` added to the `inboxId` exclusion list. 3 new tests in `lib/list.test.ts`. **NEW routes:** `app/api/threads/[id]/bin/route.ts` (`POST`, body `ThreadBinBody` = `{bin: boolean}`, gate `unifiedinbox.view` + `canOpenThread`, owner worked out server-side from the thread's own inbox and returned as `ownerUserId`) and `app/api/bin/empty/route.ts` (`POST`, body `EmptyBinBody` = `{inboxId?: string|null}`, gate **`unifiedinbox.manage`**). The empty route resolves `inboxId` against `visibleInboxIds` and refuses anything that is not an individual inbox with an owner (a shared address has no bin); null = the caller's own. It then narrows channels by `settings.hiddenChannelModules` so it destroys exactly what the folder showed, calls `binThreadIds`, and works in batches of `BATCH = 100` against `BUDGET_MS = 45_000` under `maxDuration = 60` - each batch deletes the stored objects FIRST via `storedObjectsForThreads` + `deleteMedia` (retention's rule: an orphaned object is recoverable, a row pointing at missing bytes is not) and then `deleteThreads(batch)`, so a run killed by the platform can never have parted the bytes from the rows by more than one batch. Out of budget it stops on a batch boundary and answers `{conversations, storedObjects, storedObjectFailures, more: true}`; `EmptyBinButton` then keeps the dialog up saying how many went and inviting another press rather than closing onto a folder that is still full. **It never issues an IMAP delete** - the mail stays in whatever mailbox it was collected from, and `deleteThreads` leaves the location ledger row with a null thread so the next sync does not re-collect it. There is NO timer anywhere: nothing empties a bin but that press. **UI:** NEW `icons.tsx` `BinIcon` (waste basket) and `EmptyBinIcon` (same basket, lid tilted off, nothing inside); the note on `SpamIcon` explaining why junk is NOT a basket is amended rather than deleted. NEW `components/admin/inbox/BinButton.tsx` - the basket, immediately LEFT of `SpamButton` in `ThreadActions.tsx`; no dialog (the press is reversible, like Done and Snooze), an undo toast through `useOfferUndo`, and `router.push(closeHref)` because the conversation has left every list. `ThreadActions` gains `binned` / `binOwnerName`; `ThreadPane.tsx` gains a `binState` prop kept separate from `spamState`. NEW `components/admin/inbox/EmptyBinButton.tsx` - icon button + `ConfirmDialog` (`destructive`, so the keyboard starts on Cancel; the dialog stays up holding a refusal), rendered by `Filters.tsx` in the same slot the Spam folder gives `BlockedAddresses`, between the search box and the filter icons. `ThreadListView.tsx` gains `bin` / `binOwnerName`, an empty state of its own, `deletePicked()` and `restorePicked()` (both through the existing `runOnPicked(..., {closes: true})` + an undo toast), a **Delete** button in the selection bar everywhere except the Bin - where the same slot reads **Put back** - and hides **Mark as spam** in the Bin. **RAIL:** `NavRail.tsx` gains `binCount` and a `bin` entry under Yours after `spam`; NEW `BEHIND_MORE = ['spam', 'bin']` and a **More** / **Less** button as the last row of the Yours list, which folds both away by default, carries a quiet count of what is waiting behind it, and is not drawn at all while the reader is standing in either (`current === 'spam' || current === 'bin'` forces them showing). Both stay in `orderedMine`, so a person's saved rail order keeps their slot; `sortByStoredOrder` puts an unknown key last, so an existing user gets Bin at the end of Yours. `foldersFor()` adds a **Bin** folder under every Team-inbox colleague (`bin:<inbox id>`), beside their Spam, and the `currentFolderInbox` prefix list gains `'bin'`. NEW `.uin-rail-more` / `.uin-rail-more-icon` in `inbox.css` (chevron rotates on `aria-expanded`). Shared inboxes still hang only a Sent folder - a bin belongs to a PERSON. **`InboxPanel.tsx`:** a second `countThreads({binOnly, binOwnerUserId: user.id, status:'all', unreadOnly:true})` in the same `Promise.all` as `spamCount`; `binOnly` / `binOwnerUserId` in `filters`; `status` forced to `'all'` and `showStatusTabs` false in the Bin as well as in Spam; `threadIsBinnedFor(thread.id, binOwnerId)` added to the open-conversation `Promise.all`; `folderTab` / `currentTab` / `viewTitle` / the column-title condition all learn `'bin'`; `emptyBin` passed to `Filters` only when `params.binOnly && canManage && total > 0`. **Tests:** NEW `lib/bin.test.ts` (6) and NEW `lib/bin.live.test.ts` (6, gated `RUN_INBOX_BIN_GUARDS=1`, new script `npm run test:inbox-bin`) - a real throwaway `cactus_rt_uinbin_*` database on the OVH VPS proving the seven claims the raw SQL makes, since `tsc`, `eslint`, `npm test` and the module build gate all pass a query Postgres cannot parse. README gains a **The bin** bullet and the rail list mentions More. **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `npm test` green (6693 passed, 429 skipped), `npm run test:inbox-bin` a real PASS (6/6), `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a real PASS (4/4, `unified-inbox` among the module schemas built) - owed because a module migration adds a table. **NOT verified in a browser** - visual checking is Chris's.)

Last updated: 2026-09-10 (**The conversation list grows instead of turning pages, the selection bar sticks, closing a conversation shuts its pane, and Undo now covers spam and snooze** - `unified-inbox` only, uncommitted working tree. No schema change, no new route, no new permission. **GROWING LIST:** `lib/list.ts` gains `MAX_SHOWN` (= `PER_PAGE * 20`, 500) and `shownCount(page, perPage = PER_PAGE)` = `min(MAX_SHOWN, max(1, floor(page)) * perPage)`, with 3 new tests in `lib/list.test.ts`. `components/admin/InboxPanel.tsx` now builds the conversation `filters` with `page: 1, perPage: shownCount(params.page)` instead of `page: params.page, perPage: PER_PAGE`, so `?page=3` means "the newest 75 in one list" rather than "the third 25". `ThreadListView.tsx` drops `pageCount`/`pages` and replaces the Newer / "Page N of M" / Older pager with `Showing {rows.length} of {total}` + a **Show N more** `Link` to `page+1` carrying `scroll={false}` and NO LONGER clearing `id` - nothing leaves the screen, so the open conversation stays open and the selection survives (it is already filtered against `onScreen`). At the cap the button is replaced by a sentence pointing at search. The cap exists because the address carries the helping size: an untouched `?page=100000` would otherwise be a 2.5M-row query. `.uin-pager` gains `flex-wrap: wrap`. Sent, Mentioned, Contacts and Organisations still page - deliberately untouched. **STICKY SELECTION BAR:** `.uin-bulk` in `inbox.css` gains `position: sticky; top: 0; z-index: 3`. It is a direct child of `.uin-col-scroll` (the list column's own scroller, `overflow-y: auto`), and `.uin-col-head` is OUTSIDE that box, so it pins directly under the tabs and covers nothing. z-index 3 sits above the rows; the Snooze menu is `position: fixed` via `Dropdown` and is unaffected. **CLOSING A CONVERSATION SHUTS ITS PANE:** `SpamButton` already pushed `closeHref`; done and snooze did not, so the pane sat showing a conversation the list had dropped. `ThreadActions.tsx` gains `closeWith(change, said)` - `patch(change, {refresh: false})`, raise the undo, `router.push(closeHref)` - used by the Done menu item and by `SnoozePanel.onSnooze`. `patch()` gains a `{refresh}` option so the redraw is skipped when the caller is about to navigate. "Open" and `onWake` deliberately do NOT close. In bulk, `runOnPicked` gains `{closes}`: it captures `openThreadId && picked.includes(openThreadId)` BEFORE the run (the bar empties itself) and pushes `inboxHref(base, params, {id: null})` instead of refreshing. **UNDO, LIFTED OUT OF THE PANE:** `UndoToast` was a child of `ThreadActions`, so it could never outlive the pane the press shuts - which is why spam never had one. NEW `components/admin/inbox/UndoProvider.tsx`: `UndoProvider` + `useOfferUndo()` returning `(offer: {message, undo: () => Promise<void>}) => void`, keyed per offer so a second press restarts the five seconds, and doing `router.refresh()` itself after the undo resolves. Mounted in `InboxPanel.tsx` around everything inside `.uin-page` (no extra DOM). Every `undo` is a BARE fetch, never a call back into the component that raised it - that component is usually unmounted by then. Now offered by: single done and single snooze (`ThreadActions.wasAt()` captures status + `snoozeUntil` at press time), single spam and spam+block (`SpamButton.offerToPutBack(blocked)`, and `moveAndBlock` now tracks whether the block request actually succeeded so the sentence is true), bulk done / open again / snooze (`ThreadListView.closePicked()` + `whereTheyStood()`, one restore change per conversation because a picked pile is rarely all in one state), and bulk spam with or without blocking (`takeOutOfSpam(ids)`). **Undo never unblocks a sender** - the bulk list is deliberately never told who was already blocked (see `blockable`), so reopening every door could silently readmit somebody turned away weeks ago; the toast says the senders stay blocked. Bulk mark as read/unread get no toast: neither moves anything and the opposite button is beside them. **RAIL AUTO-EXPAND:** `NavRail.tsx` `isOpened()` forced a branch open only when `current` was one of its FOLDERS (`sent:<id>`, `drafts:<id>`, `mentions:<id>`, `spam:<id>`); it now also opens when `current === id`, so pressing a shared or team inbox shows its Sent / Drafts / Mentioned / Spam without hunting for the twist arrow that only appears on hover. That force made the arrow dead on the row you stand in, so a new `shut` state `{where: current, id}` records one hand-collapsed branch scoped to the entry it was made on - collapse works everywhere, and arriving at the address again re-opens it. Derived at render, no effect (`react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` forbids the effect form). Wiki `Unified-Inbox.md`: bulk-actions section gains sticky bar + growing list; the done-undo paragraph is rewritten to cover all three closing actions, bulk and the block caveat, plus a new paragraph on the pane closing; the rail table and the covering-somebody's-post section say pressing an address opens its folders. **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `npm test` green.)

Last updated: 2026-09-10 (**Snooze a whole pile at once, and a shared address's signature stops claiming it signs every reply** - `unified-inbox` only, uncommitted working tree. No schema change, no new route. **BULK SNOOZE:** `components/admin/inbox/ThreadListView.tsx` gains a `Dropdown` + the existing `SnoozePanel` in the selection bar, between **Open again** and **Mark as spam**. Answers go through the existing `applyToPicked({status:'snoozed', snoozeUntil})`, which is one `PATCH /api/m/unified-inbox/threads/[id]` per conversation, settled not raced. Unlike the other bar buttons it is NOT behind an `offer.*` test - re-timing something already asleep is a real change - and it deliberately carries no "bring it back": **Open again** beside it already does that. Last updated: 2026-09-10 (**A colleague's draft can now be sent out for them, untouched** - `unified-inbox` only, uncommitted working tree. No schema change. **THE GAP:** the Drafts folder under a colleague's name opened a read-only view whose foot said "This is Sarah's writing, so it is here to read only. Only they can finish it or send it." - so a finished quote sitting on the address of somebody on a fortnight's leave waited for them. **THE RULE:** editing stays the author's (`canEditDraft` unchanged); SENDING splits off as a narrower right. NEW `canSendDraftForOwner(draft, inbox, mayReplyFromInbox)` in `modules/unified-inbox/lib/drafts.ts` + 6 tests in `lib/drafts.test.ts`: individual inbox with an owner, draft authored by that owner AND filed on that inbox, and the reader may send from it (`canReplyToInbox`, the reply grant, not the view one). **NEW `lib/draft-send.ts`** - `postDraft(draft, thread, {sentByUserId, idempotencyKey})` and `draftSendingInboxId()`: the how-a-stored-draft-becomes-a-send half, lifted verbatim out of `sendOneScheduled` in `lib/scheduled-send.ts` and checking NO rights of its own. `lib/scheduled-send.ts` now keeps only its own rights checks (asked of the AUTHOR) and calls it. **NEW route `app/api/drafts/[id]/send/route.ts`** (`POST`, `unifiedinbox.reply`, `maxDuration = 60`, body `SendDraftForBody` = `{inboxId}` in `lib/validation.ts`): `canReplyToInbox` -> `getInboxAudience` -> `getDraftInInbox(id, inbox.ownerUserId, inboxId)` (the author comes from the ADDRESS, never from the request) -> `canSendDraftForOwner` -> `canOpenThread` + `visibleChannelKeys` where there is a conversation -> `standDownScheduledDraft` (409 if the queue already has it) -> `postDraft` with `idempotencyKey` `on-behalf-<draftId>`, distinct from the queue's `scheduled-<draftId>` -> `deleteDraft` + `applyFollowUpAfterSend`. No body is read from the request, so nothing can leave saying something the author did not type. `sentByUserId` is whoever pressed it, so **Sent** credits them; the signature is unchanged (an individual address signs as its owner whoever presses Send - `lib/send.ts`). **NEW `components/admin/inbox/SendDraftForColleague.tsx`** - button + `ConfirmDialog`, shows the server's own refusal sentence verbatim, `router.refresh()` on success. `DraftReadView.tsx` takes new `inboxId` and `canSend` props and draws either the button or the old read-only sentence; `InboxPanel.tsx` passes `sendableIds.includes(folderInbox.id)`. NEW `.uin-draft-read-foot` / `.uin-draft-read-actions` in `inbox.css`. Comments in `lib/db.ts`, `NavRail.tsx` and `DraftListView.tsx` that asserted "only the author may send" corrected. Wiki `Unified-Inbox.md` updated in Drafts and in the colleague-folders section. **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `npm test` green (6681 passed, 423 skipped). No raw SQL added, no schema change, so no backup round-trip or ledger guard was owed.)

Last updated: 2026-09-10 (**Every admin screen tells you which one it is** - core, uncommitted working tree: module admin screens' own page titles were being discarded by the route that renders them, and most core admin screens had no title at all, so a browser full of admin tabs read the site name over and over. See *core - every admin screen names itself* below. Previously: **Run a scheduled job on the spot, campaigns move while anyone reads their post, and Google can be told which delivery group a product belongs to** - core plus `unified-inbox`, `product-attributes-for-shop` and `google-shopping-for-shop`, uncommitted working tree. **CORE, RUN NOW:** NEW `lib/cron/run.ts` holds `callCronJob()`, `describeFailure()` and `cronCallEnvironment()` - the fetch, the bearer token and the reading of a failure's own words, lifted out of `app/api/cron/dispatch/route.ts` (which now calls it, unchanged in behaviour) so a job run by hand is the same job run the same way. NEW `app/api/admin/cron/run/route.ts` (`POST`, `config.manage`, `maxDuration = 60`): checks the path is a job this install actually declares, stamps `CronRun.lastRunAt` BEFORE the call exactly as the dispatcher does, waits for the job and writes back `lastStatus`/`lastError`. Running early does not move the schedule - the stamp is what the dispatcher reads, so a nightly job run at four is next due the following night. NEW exports `runCronJob()` and `describeRunResult()` in `components/admin/CronScheduleField.tsx`, so a module hosting the control on its own settings tab gets the same button. `app/cactus-admin/config/ScheduledJobsClient.tsx` gains a Run now column, one job at a time, and updates that row's Last run from what came back. No schema change. **UNIFIED-INBOX, THE CAMPAIGN CLOCK MOVES TO THE RAIL:** a campaign only ever advanced while somebody had the Campaigns screen open, so on a five-minute site it crawled while its owner sat in the inbox all day. NEW `components/admin/inbox/campaigns/CampaignPulse.tsx` - renders nothing, `POST`s `/api/m/unified-inbox/admin/campaigns/tick` every 30s while the tab is visible (and on becoming visible again), skips a tick while one is in flight, and announces what went out on `window` as `uin:campaign-tick`. Mounted beside `NavRail` in `components/admin/InboxPanel.tsx`, so it rides every inbox screen; gated on `unifiedinbox.campaigns` and on NEW `countRunningCampaigns()` in `lib/campaigns/store.ts`, so a site with nothing running mounts nothing. `CampaignTicker.tsx` no longer keeps a timer of its own - it listens for that event and says what has just gone, which is one tick where there were two. No schema change, no new route: the tick route already existed. **PRODUCT-ATTRIBUTES-FOR-SHOP, A GENERIC SEAM:** NEW `lib/attribute-values-provider.ts` and NEW extension point contribution `shop.product-attribute-values` (`productAttributesValuesProvider`, `listAttributes()` + `valuesFor(attributeId, productIds)`). Read-only, names no consumer and carries no consumer's shape. **GOOGLE-SHOPPING-FOR-SHOP, `shipping_label`:** NEW migration `007_shipping_label.sql` adds `gsf_settings.shipping_label_attribute_id` (TEXT, null = off, which is the default). NEW `lib/product-labels.ts` reads the point above through core's public extension-point registry, exactly as `delivery-timing.ts` does, so a shop without an attributes module simply has no provider. NEW `fitShippingLabel()` in `lib/feed-xml.ts` cuts a label to Google's 100 characters and appends a four-character fingerprint of the whole original, so two labels differing only past the cut do not collapse into one delivery group in Merchant Center; `FeedItem.shippingLabel` renders as `g:shipping_label`. `lib/feed-data.ts` resolves one label per item in a single batched pass - a variation's own value first, its parent's where it has none. Settings tab gains "Group your products for delivery rates" under Delivery, and the settings API carries `shippingLabelAttributeId`. **GOOGLE-SHOPPING-FOR-SHOP, `return_policy_label`:** NEW migration `008_return_policy_labels.sql` adds `gsf_settings.return_policy_labels_enabled` (BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false - off, because a label naming a policy Merchant Center does not hold is silently ignored and the item is judged returnable after all). NEW `lib/return-policy.ts` (`returnPolicyLabelFor(child, parent)`) + `lib/return-policy.test.ts` (8 tests): the label IS the shop's own `non_returnable_note`, so the sentence a customer reads and the policy Google applies cannot drift apart. Child-then-parent inheritance is shop's own `resolveReturnable()`, not a second rule - a listing marked bespoke labels all its variations, and a child carrying its own flag wins in both directions. Non-returnable with no note falls back to shop's `NON_RETURNABLE_DEFAULT_NOTE` rather than no label, since no label means returnable. Reuses `fitShippingLabel()` for Google's 100-character ceiling. `FeedItem.returnPolicyLabel` renders as `g:return_policy_label` in `lib/feed-xml.ts`; `lib/feed-data.ts` adds `returnable`/`non_returnable_note` to the parent and child raw SELECTs and resolves inline (both rows already in hand, no extra query), standalone items answering as both halves. Settings tab gains "Tell Google which things cannot be sent back"; settings API carries `returnPolicyLabelsEnabled`. **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `npm test` green (6656 passed), `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a real PASS against throwaway OVH databases - required because a module migration added a column.)

Last updated: 2026-09-10 (**The away card now says the owner's own words, and stopped lying about reply time when the switch says Offline** - `live-chat` only, uncommitted working tree, migration `005_away_message.sql` (mirrored into `001_initial.sql`). **THE BUG:** Chatwoot seeds every new inbox with a Mon-Fri 9-5 business-hours row set and never removes it, even with business hours switched off (this module never turns them on). Its widget reads those rows BEFORE it reads the agent's actual availability, so inside 9-5 it printed "Typically replies in a few minutes" whatever the Online/Offline switch said - precisely the wrong line for a visitor arriving while nobody is on. The rows cannot be deleted over the API, only rewritten, so NEW `closeInboxWorkingHours()` in `lib/chatwoot.ts` marks all seven closed instead; harmless since hours stay off. Applied once per install via NEW `closeWorkingHoursOnce()` in `app/api/admin/availability/route.ts`'s `GET` (guarded by NEW `lc_settings.working_hours_closed`, a failure just retries next open) and unconditionally on fresh provisioning in `app/api/admin/provision/route.ts`. **THE SECOND HALF:** the widget's own away wording was hardcoded English ("We are away right now..."), not the owner's. NEW `lc_settings.away_message` (TEXT, default in both the migration and `DEFAULT_AWAY_MESSAGE` in `lib/settings.ts` so a pre-005 row still reads as a sentence) threads through `getLiveChatConfig`/`updateSettings`/the settings API/the widget boot route to `WidgetLoader.tsx`'s `unavailableMessage` (Chatwoot's own away-card copy) and the bubble tooltip when away. NEW settings field "What to say when you are away" in `SettingsTab.tsx`. `WidgetLoader.tsx` also no longer jumps a visitor straight to the messages pane (`jumpToMessages()`) when `info.online === false` - away, the away/home card is exactly the screen that should show first. **Gates:** `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, `npm test` green. No backup-roundtrip run for this entry specifically, but core's release below carries a clean gate run covering the whole tree. **NOT verified in a browser** - visual checking is Chris's.)

Last updated: 2026-09-10 (**A colleague's Drafts folder is now readable by whoever covers their post, and the rail counts what is still open rather than what is still unread** - `unified-inbox` only, uncommitted working tree. No schema change. **PART ONE - THE FOLDER.** A "Team inboxes" address (an individual inbox owned by another person) opened out into Sent, Mentioned and Spam, all three of them THEIRS, plus a "Your drafts" row that showed the READER's own writing on that address and only appeared when there was some. So the one folder that was not the colleague's wore the colleague's heading, and the answer half-written and waiting - the exact thing that makes the same customer get written to twice - was invisible to whoever was covering. Drafts under a colleague's name is now theirs, always offered like the three beside it, and READ-ONLY: `canReadDraft`/`canEditDraft` in `modules/unified-inbox/lib/drafts.ts` are unchanged and still answer "only the author", so nothing on the screen will finish or send one. **NEW QUERIES** in `lib/db.ts`: `countDraftsByInboxOwner()` (no reader argument at all - it JOINs `uin_drafts` to `uin_inboxes` on `kind = 'individual' AND owner_user_id = author_user_id`, so a number can never be one person's writing under another's heading) replaces `countDraftsByInbox(userId)`; `getDraftInInbox(id, authorUserId, inboxId)` puts BOTH halves in the WHERE for the read-only view. `listDrafts` is unchanged - the panel simply passes the folder's owner instead of the viewer, and `folderOwnerId` (already used by Spam and Mentioned) is what settles who that is. **NEW COMPONENT** `components/admin/inbox/DraftReadView.tsx` - the pane beside the list: subject, To/Cc, attachment, schedule notice, body. Bcc is deliberately omitted (a blind copy is a fact its author kept off the message). The body goes in as TEXT via `draftBodyText` and the plain-text `MessageText` renderer; nothing here is ever set as HTML. `DraftListView` takes an `ownerName` prop: set, the rows link to `?draft=<id>` with NO `compose`, so the panel draws the reading view rather than a writing box that would refuse to save. New CSS block `.uin-draft-read*` in `inbox.css`. **PART TWO - THE COUNTERS.** `unreadCounts` -> `openCounts` and `unreadAssignedElsewhere` -> `openAssignedElsewhere`, both now `t."status" = 'open'` in place of `t."unread" = true AND t."status" <> 'done'`. The rail badge and the list behind it now agree, because clicking an address lands on the Open tab; the old badge emptied itself the moment somebody glanced at the list and said nothing about the work left. Snoozed is deliberately out - it has been decided about. The Spam count is untouched and is still unread junk (a bin nobody empties would otherwise sit at a permanent 47). `Count`'s screen-reader default word is now "open". **TESTS:** `lib/sent-list.live.test.ts` rewritten around the owner-scoped count and given cases for a draft written on somebody ELSE's address and for `getDraftInInbox`'s two halves; `lib/own-desk.live.test.ts` now asserts that READING a conversation leaves it on the desk and FINISHING it takes it off. Ran green against real throwaway databases on the VPS: `RUN_INBOX_SENT_GUARDS` 30/30, `RUN_INBOX_SPAM_GUARDS` + `RUN_INBOX_MERGE_GUARDS` + `RUN_INBOX_ADDRESSED` 44/44, `RUN_INBOX_OWN_DESK` + `RUN_INBOX_DISCUSSIONS` + `RUN_INBOX_REOPEN_GUARDS` 21/21.)

Last updated: 2026-09-10 (**A parcel's own courier now reports the round, the driver and the history, and the button out to them is labelled for what only they can do** - `shop` only, uncommitted working tree. No core change. **THE PROBLEM:** the tracking added for Multidrop reports one thing, a stage off a seven-step timeline, and every other carrier reports rather more. Verified live against DW000182 (Dynamic Office Seating, DPD, out for delivery 10 September): DPD's anonymous feed carries 15 fields and their session feed about 90. **MIGRATION `modules/shop/migrations/049_carrier_scan_tracking.sql`** (new; mirrored into `001_initial.sql` in place, per the fresh-install rule), all idempotent: `shp_shipments.tracking_short_code TEXT`, `tracking_events JSONB`, `delivery_window_from`/`_to TIMESTAMP(3)`, `stop_number`/`stops_completed`/`stops_total`/`minutes_to_stop INTEGER`, `driver_name TEXT`, plus partial index `shp_shipments_tracking_poll_code_idx` - without it a parcel whose only feed is a short code would never be polled, since the existing poll index is `WHERE tracking_url IS NOT NULL`. `listShipmentsForTrackingPoll` widened to match. **THE SEAM:** NEW `lib/tracking/reading.ts` (`TrackingReading`, `TrackingEvent`, `trackingEventsSchema` - jsonb is CHECKED on the way out, never cast, because a column outlives the build that wrote it), NEW `lib/tracking/read-parcel.ts` dispatching on `trackingSource`, and the cron no longer names a reader: `app/api/cron/delivery-tracking/route.ts` calls `readParcelTracking` and writes down what comes back. `courierIsPolled` is now `!== 'none'` rather than a list of sources - the failure a list causes is silent and looks exactly like a courier's site being down. **TWO NEW READERS.** `lib/tracking/gfs.ts`: Global Freight Solutions are the broker Dynamic book through, and their page is the RAW CARRIER SCAN FEED - `PRESTON` and `HUB 5 - HINCKLEY` where DPD's own guest page says `DPD`, and `OUT FOR DELIVERY, ETA: 11:41 - 12:41` where DPD say "your parcel will be with you today between...". Scan codes are a short stable vocabulary; marketing sentences are not. **`ConsNumber` + `ParcelNumber` + `Carrier` is the whole key** - the `custid` on the links suppliers send makes no difference (verified) and a shop should never need a supplier's account number; dropping `ConsNumber` is a 500. Their dates are day-first (`10/9/2026 09:41`) and are parsed by hand, because `new Date` reads that as the 9th of October. **`lib/tracking/dpd.ts`**: pure parsing plus zod schemas held deliberately loose (somebody else's feed; a reader that threw on a new key would stop tracking every parcel at once). The stage is the newest EVENT text, NOT `trackingStatusCurrent` - that sentence names the shipper, the driver and the timeslot, so no two parcels produce a string a setting could match. `parcelStatusType` stayed `0` through the entire journey including out-for-delivery and nothing keys off it. Driver's first name is read from their own `<SPAN class="REDTEXT">` markup rather than from the words around it. **`lib/tracking/dpd-session.ts`**: `GET /v1/createSession?parcelCode=<shortCode>&origin=d` returns a 302 whose `set-cookie` is the session - `redirect: 'manual'` matters, following it loses the header - and one cookie is minted per poll and handed to the route call rather than minted twice. **WHAT THE SHORT CODE IS:** the tail of the `www.dpd.co.uk/d/<code>` link DPD email, and it is a SESSION KEY, not an identifier. `createSession` refuses the parcel code, the parcel number and the consignment number alike (400), so it cannot be derived - it has to be pasted. With it: `deliveryDepot.route.stop` gives the window as real instants, `stopNumber` and `estimatedMinsToStop`, and `/v1/routes/{routeCode}` gives `completedDeliveryStops` of `totalDeliveryStops` and the driver. **The driver endpoint wants `{depotCode}*{driverCode}`** (`0045*DR62251`); the bare driver code returns 403, which reads like a permissions problem and is not. **NO LIVE MAP FOR DPD, and it is not coming:** their other way in is `POST /v1/login` with the delivery postcode plus a `g-recaptcha-response` header (`403 "The response parameter is missing."` without it), and no endpoint probed carries a van position - `stop.addressPoint` was null throughout. **CONFIG:** `trackingSource` gains `'gfs'` and `'dpd'`; NEW `gfsCarrier` (default `DPD` - GFS are the booking company, not the van); NEW `trackingLinkLabel` / `trackingLinkHint`, both defaulting to `''` so nothing changes for a shop that never touches them. That last pair exists because the REASON to click through stopped being tracking: on a followed courier the customer has just read the timeline, the window and the driver, so "Track your parcel" sends them out for what they already have and buries the safe place, the neighbour and the change of date that only the courier can do. **CUSTOMER-FACING:** NEW `lib/tracking/live-line.ts` - "Mozam has 2 more drops to make before yours. About 15 minutes away." **Counted towards THIS parcel, never across the driver's day, and the bar with it** - `stopsCompleted / stopNumber`, NOT `/ stopsTotal`. Found on DW000182 in front of Chris: 32 of 98 drew a third of a bar while the van was two drops away, which reads as "not for hours" to the one person the bar is for. The day's total is still recorded for the admin screens and is never said to a customer. **The estimate has the time since the last poll taken off it** and floors at "any minute now" rather than counting backwards; the bar is drawn only from the two stop counts, never from the estimate, so it cannot disagree with the sentence above it. NEW `components/public/ParcelTracking.tsx` (live lines, bar, and the courier's history in a closed `<details>`), rendered on the account order page - the guest tracker redirects into that page once the postcode is proved, so there is one surface, not two. Event times are printed by cutting the stored string up rather than by making a `Date` of it: the readers store the courier's own reckoning with no offset, and turning it into an instant would apply the SERVER's timezone to a timestamp that never had one.
