Last updated: 2026-09-06 (**Reviews go to Google, and Google asks your customers for one** - `google-shopping-for-shop` + `reviews-for-shop`, uncommitted working tree. Two halves of Google's review programme, and neither needs a core change.

**The review feed.** `reviews-for-shop` gains `lib/published-reviews-provider.ts`, registered on a new vendor-neutral extension point **`shop.product-reviews`** (`reviewsPublishedReviewsProvider({limit, offset})`): published reviews of ACTIVE, non-catalogue-hidden products, newest live first, ordered by `COALESCE(published_at, created_at) DESC, id ASC` so paging cannot repeat or skip a row, each carrying `ratingMax`, `verifiedPurchase`, `invited` (a `LEFT JOIN rvw_invites` on order+product) and the order number. No email, no IP, no shop reply. It is a PUBLISHER, so gsf never imports it - the same optional-companion seam `lib/delivery-timing.ts` already uses, which is what keeps the build green on a site without the reviews module.

gsf's side: `lib/reviews-source.ts` (registry lookup, per-row validation, paged read capped at 5000/500 - and the paging follows a page on what the provider RETURNED, not on what survived checking, or one malformed review would truncate the feed), `lib/review-feed-xml.ts` (pure renderer for Google's product reviews schema **2.3**, written off the XSD itself: every complexType there is an `xs:sequence`, so element ORDER is load-bearing, and `nonEmptyStringType` means an absent value leaves the element out rather than writing an empty one; C0 control characters are stripped because one of them in one review invalidates the whole document), `lib/review-feed-data.ts` (brand/GTIN/MPN off `gsf_product_data` and the product's own barcode, product URLs through shop's `productUrl`, publisher name and favicon from core's `resolveBranding` - and a product EXCLUDED from the product feed has its reviews left out too). SKUs are deliberately never sent, the same line the product feed holds.

**The address.** `/google-shopping/feed.xml?key=<token>&content=reviews`, on the existing route. Core dispatches the literal segment `feed.xml` and nothing else (`app/(public)/[slug]/feed.xml/route.ts`), so a second public file name would have meant a core change; a query parameter is what Merchant Center's scheduled fetch takes anyway. The reviews branch skips the price test (reviews quote no prices, so a quote-only shop may publish them) and has its own switch, `reviews_feed_enabled`.

**Google Customer Reviews.** New Puck block **`GoogleCustomerReviews`** on layout type `shopConfirmation` (`components/CustomerReviewsBlock.tsx` + `.rsc.tsx` + `CustomerReviewsOptIn.tsx`), auto-placed on first install. The RSC half reads settings and whether the site's banner carries a `marketing` category; the client half reads `orderNumber` and the signed receipt token `t` off the URL, fetches **`GET /api/m/google-shopping-for-shop/public/customer-reviews`** and renders Google's `surveyoptin` dialog. Order of operations is load-bearing: `window.renderOptIn` is defined BEFORE `apis.google.com/js/platform.js?onload=renderOptIn` is appended, or the callback fires against nothing. `window.__cactusGcrRendered` stops two copies of the block asking twice.

The payload is built server-side (`lib/customer-reviews.ts`) and returns null - the same empty answer for every refusal, so probing cannot tell the cases apart - unless the token verifies against that order (shop's `verifyOrderReceiptToken`), the order is PAID and a Merchant Center account number is set. The estimated delivery date is `paidAt ?? createdAt` plus the slowest line's handling + transit days from the delivery-timing provider, falling back to the shop-wide `customer_reviews_delivery_days`, counted Monday-Friday (`addWorkingDays`, bank holidays deliberately not modelled). GTINs ride along where known. The email leaves the server only to the confirmation page of the order it belongs to, and the answer is `no-store, private`.

**Migrations:** `005_product_reviews_feed.sql` (`reviews_feed_enabled`) and `006_customer_reviews_optin.sql` (`customer_reviews_enabled`, `customer_reviews_style`, `customer_reviews_delivery_days`), both `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`. BOOLEAN, TEXT and INTEGER only, so `isSupportedUdtName` needs no new branch. **Manifest:** `cspOrigins` script `https://apis.google.com`, img `ssl.gstatic.com`/`www.gstatic.com`/`www.google.com`, frame `www.google.com`/`apis.google.com`.

**Existing installs place the block by hand.** `autoPlaceBlocks` runs once, behind `layoutsSeededAt`, so a site that already has this module will not have the marker put on its confirmation layout - the settings tab says so in as many words.

**Gates:** `npm run typecheck` and `eslint` clean; 55 module tests green, including a new `review-feed-xml.test.ts` (element order asserted, not assumed), `reviews-source.test.ts` (13 seam cases) and `customer-reviews-dates.test.ts`. The provider's SQL was RUN against the live Deskwell database read-only - `tsc`, `eslint` and the build gate all pass raw SQL Postgres would reject, so it was executed rather than eyeballed.)

Last updated: 2026-09-05 (**Both CSV exports ask which columns you want first** - `shop` and `shop-variations`, uncommitted working tree. The Products **Export CSV** button and the Variations **Download variations CSV** button were plain `<Link>`s to a route that always wrote every column; an owner who wanted product code, categories and nothing else got a 48-column file to prune by hand. New shared client component **`modules/shop/components/admin/ExportColumnsModal.tsx`** - generic, knows nothing about either format: it takes `groups`, a `storageKey`, an optional `requiredKeys`, and a `buildHref(keys)` that turns the ticks into a download url. The choice is kept in **localStorage** (`shop.products.export.columns`, `shop-variations.export.columns`), read once in the `useState` initialiser rather than an effect so the boxes do not visibly re-tick, and every read and write is in a try/catch with 'everything ticked' as the fallback - it is a per-person habit, not shop configuration, so no table and no migration. Shop side: `buildExportCsv(rows, columns?)` gained an optional subset (defaults to `CSV_COLUMNS`, so the Google-Sheet mirror and every other caller are untouched), `GET /api/m/shop/admin/products/export` now reads `?columns=` through the existing `pickCsvColumns` - unknown names dropped, empty falling back to the whole format, the same lenient rule the import-template route uses - and `lib/csv.ts` gained **`CSV_COLUMN_LABELS`** (a human name per column) and **`CSV_COLUMN_GROUPS`** (nine groups: the basics, prices and VAT, stock, catalogue, words and pictures, size and weight, pre-orders, downloads, related and upsells). A filtered sheet still re-imports as long as `sku` or `slug` is in it - `headerMatchesUpdateFormat` is satisfied - which is asserted rather than assumed. Variations side is harder because half its header is dynamic: `Option N`/`Value N` grow with the widest product and the variant-field providers append their own columns, neither knowable without building the whole grid (which IS the export). So new client-safe **`modules/shop-variations/lib/export-columns.ts`** offers the 16 fixed columns by name plus **two group tokens**, `__options__` and `__extras__`, one tick each; `exportVariationsCsv(columns?)` builds a **`kinds` array positionally alongside the header** and filters on that, never on the label, so a provider column called 'Price' can never switch the real Price column. **`Variant ID` is a required tick** - it is what the importer and the Sheet Pull match a row back on, and without it a renamed option value reads as a different variant. It cannot live in `lib/csv.ts` (that file imports Prisma and the picker is a client component), which is also why `PRICE_TYPE_COLUMNS` is repeated there and pinned to the real header by a test. New tests: 4 in `modules/shop/lib/csv-roundtrip.test.ts` (groups cover `CSV_COLUMNS` exactly once, a label for every column, subset written in format order not caller order, empty falls back to all) and 6 in new `modules/shop-variations/lib/csv-export-columns.test.ts` (the fixed list matches what the exporter actually writes, groups cover fixed + the two tokens, cells stay aligned with the filtered header, the two blocks come whole). `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint .` clean, 5351 tests green. No schema, no SQL, no migration. Wiki: `Shop.md` and `Shop-variations.md` both updated.)

Last updated: 2026-09-05 (**Half of what the build uploads was four databases we do not use** - core working tree. Measured against a real build's own file traces: 261 functions, 462 MB of distinct files, and **29.8 GB written across the functions**, which is the pile Vercel hashes and uploads in "Deploying outputs" - 80-120s of every deploy. 14 GB of that, 46.9%, was `@prisma/client`'s base64-inlined WebAssembly query engine AND query compiler for **all five** dialects it supports - CockroachDB, MySQL, SQL Server, SQLite and PostgreSQL - copied whole into all 258 functions at 53 MB each. None of it can run here: the wasm client is for Edge and for driver adapters, and `prisma/schema.prisma` declares neither (no `driverAdapters` preview feature, no `engineType`), no route sets `runtime = 'edge'`, and `proxy.ts` is Node-only by design. Every query is served by the native engine, `node_modules/.prisma/client/libquery_engine-<platform>.node`, which is traced separately. New **`outputFileTracingExcludes`** in `next.config.ts`, keyed `'**'` (route globs, `contains` semantics, same matcher as `outputFileTracingIncludes` - see `next/dist/build/collect-build-traces.js`), drops `query_engine_bg.*.wasm-base64.*`, `query_compiler_bg.*.wasm-base64.*` and `.prisma/client/query_engine_bg.wasm`. Replayed against the existing traces: 5,418 entries and 13.97 GB gone, the native engine still present in all 258 functions, payload 29.8 GB -> 15.8 GB. Separately, new leaf module **`lib/media/worker-url.ts`** holds `workerUrl()` - an environment-variable read with no imports of its own, which had been sitting in `lib/media/upload.ts` alongside `sharp`, `@aws-sdk/client-s3`, the glTF optimiser (`draco3d`, `@gltf-transform`) and `jsdom` via `lib/sanitize`. `lib/media/asset-token.ts`, which signs the media urls on **every public page**, imported it from there and dragged the lot along; it now takes the leaf. `upload.ts` re-exports `workerUrl` so all existing callers are untouched. **This does not by itself take sharp out of the public functions**: `lib/modules/extension-points.public.ts` also imports `product-attributes-for-shop`'s `option-source-provider`, which statically imports `generateSwatchCopies` -> `lib/media/renditions.ts` -> `sharp`, so the 2 GB that path is worth needs a module-side change as well. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint .`, client-graph check and 5,332 tests all clean.)

Last updated: 2026-09-05 (**The build cache stops being thrown away, and two packages stop being carried for nothing** - core working tree, plus `gazette`, `boards` and `space-planner-for-shop`. Vercel invalidates the whole build cache over 1.5 GB, and a WARM build here came out at 1.66-2.08 GB against a cold one's 1.33 GB, so 5 of the last 13 deploys started from a clean state and took 367s instead of 170s. New `scripts/prune-build-cache.mjs`, spawned by `scripts/next-build.mjs` after a successful build, takes 196 MB of build-only packages out of node_modules always and another 334 MB when it has to, deciding from the file traces `next build` has just written rather than from a list of guesses, and dropping the Turbopack cache only as a last resort because it is the dearest thing in there. `@sparticuz/chromium` became `@sparticuz/chromium-min` (66 MB in node_modules and 66 MB in every module-API function, down to 72 KB and a pack fetched to /tmp on a cold print), with the browser hunt moved into one new `lib/documents/chromium.ts` that core's PDF renderer and the space planner both call. And shiki left the Gazette post editor's client bundle: `GazetteCode.tsx` was importing the whole default bundle, ~346 grammars, into a `'use client'` editor that renders plain text, so the highlighting moved to a new `GazetteCodeRsc.tsx` built over the 18 grammars the block actually offers, and `boards` got the same split - 254 grammar files traced down to 29, and no client chunk carrying the library at all. `tsc --noEmit` clean, client graph clean, a real Turbopack build compiled in 50s.)

Last updated: 2026-09-04 (**A guest can open their own order by giving the delivery postcode, and every order email links to it** - `shop`, uncommitted working tree. A shopper who checked out without an account had no order page at all: no tracking, no invoice, and no way to correct the company name their accounts department had just queried - all of which arrived at the shop by telephone instead. They now prove themselves with the **delivery postcode**, typed any way at all (`normalisePostcode` strips everything that is not a letter or a digit, so 'E1 1AA', 'e1 1aa' and 'E11AA' are one postcode and 'B29 7QB' is not), and the **order number** likewise (`orderNumberCandidates` accepts 'DW000172', 'dw000172', '000172' and plain '172' against the shop's own `orderNumberPrefix`, and offers what was typed as a candidate too for a shop numbering its own way; `findOrdersByNumberCandidates` in `lib/db/orders.ts` looks them all up at once and refuses to guess when two are real). New pure libs: `lib/order-lookup.ts` and `lib/order-tracking.ts` (`signOrderTrackingToken`/`verifyOrderTrackingToken` in their own `track:` namespace, `orderTrackingRootSlug`, `orderTrackingBasePath`, `orderTrackingPath`, `orderTrackingUrl`), both with unit tests. Proof is kept in a signed httpOnly cookie **`cactus_shop_order_access`** (`lib/guest-order-access.ts` - HMAC over a list of order ids plus a signed expiry, 30 days, at most 10 orders, newest first; declared in the manifest's new `cacheBypassCookies` so a page carrying one is never made shareable). Who may see an order is now ONE rule in `lib/order-viewer.ts` (`orderViewerFor` + `resolveOrderViewer`), asked by the page, the receipt and all six routes through `lib/order-route-access.ts` (`requireOrderAccess`) - member-and-owner, or guest-with-cookie, and everything else is the same 404. `getMemberOrderDetail` splits into `loadOrderDetail(orderId)` plus a thin ownership wrapper. A verified guest gets **exactly** what a member gets: `app/public/shop/account/orders/[id]/page.tsx` renders the postcode gate in place when nobody has proved themselves, then the full page outside `MemberAccountShell` with a 'Track another order' back link and an inline account offer (`components/public/GuestOrderAccountOffer.tsx`, core's `RegisterForm` prefilled with the order email, behind the existing `postPurchaseAccountPrompt` switch); the receipt page and `api/member/orders/[id]` (GET+PATCH), `/billing`, `/pay`, `/pay/confirm`, `/requests` and `api/member/requests/[id]` all go through `requireOrderAccess`. `withdrawRequest` is now scoped to the **order** rather than the member, which also fixes a member who registered after ordering being unable to withdraw their own request. New pages `app/public/shop/track-order/page.tsx` (exports `TrackOrderPageView`) and `app/public/shop/track-order/[orderNumber]/page.tsx` (the email's landing page: signed-in owner or proved guest is redirected straight to the order, a genuine token names the order and asks only for the postcode, anything else falls back to the plain form), new client `components/public/OrderAccessForm.tsx`, server `components/public/OrderAccessGate.tsx`, and `components/public/track-order-css.ts` (`TRACK_ORDER_CSS`, `sot-*` prefix, injected `<style>`, every colour a token). New public route **`POST /api/m/shop/public/orders/track`** - one wording for every failure so failures cannot be used to find real order numbers. Brute force is defended by a new table **`shp_order_access_attempts`** (`migrations/038_order_access_attempts.sql`, mirrored in `001_initial.sql`, added to the manifest `teardown`) and `lib/db/order-access.ts`: eight wrong postcodes on one order locks it for an hour, the count resets after an hour of quiet, and it is keyed on the ORDER because the existing in-memory IP limiter is per-instance and keyed on something the guesser chooses. New config: **`guestOrderTrackingEnabled`** (default true) and **`orderTrackingRootSlug`** (default `track-order`), both on Settings -> Shop -> Checkout -> Order history; the root slug is claimed through `lib/root-slug.ts` and `app/root/[slug]/page.tsx`, which now branches tracker-first before the product page, and is added to `lib/robots.ts` alongside `/shop/track-order`. Emails: new merge tags **`orderUrl`/`hasOrderUrl`** on all 13 customer-facing order templates with a default 'Keep track of your order at ...' line; filled centrally in `notifyOrderCustomer` (so every email AND every SMS carries it and none can be missed) plus `orderTrackingVars` in `lib/email.ts` for the two that send direct - the credit note and the reissued invoice. **Owner copy overrides do not gain the new line automatically** - an edited template keeps its own wording and needs `{{orderUrl}}` adding by hand. Verified against real Postgres: new `modules/shop/lib/db/order-access.live.test.ts` (9 tests, added to `npm run test:ledger-guards`) exercises the lockout arithmetic, the window reset, the sweep, the candidate lookup and the order-scoped withdraw; `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` passed.)

Last updated: 2026-09-04 (**The customer's own order page rebuilt around what they came for** - `shop` v0.1.392, released. `app/public/shop/account/orders/[id]/page.tsx` had grown one `Section` at a time - pay online, proforma, invoice address, purchase order number, items, parcels, downloads, totals, refunds, addresses, requests - into eleven identical `.card` blocks in one column, every one the same size and weight, with the paperwork strung off the 'Placed 3 August 2026' line as middot-separated links (five wrapped lines on an order with two invoices and a credit note). Rebuilt in priority order: header (number, status badge, date, item count, total), then a **four-step progress rail** (Ordered, Paid, Dispatched, Complete), then anything still to DO (money owed, an open cancel/return request) as tinted callouts, then the receipt - items AND totals in ONE card - then everything else as **paired cards, two columns on desktop and one on a phone**. New files, all in `modules/shop`: `components/public/order-detail-css.ts` (`ORDER_DETAIL_CSS`, `sod-*` prefix, injected `<style>` exactly like `order-confirmation-css.ts` - never a core `globals.css` edit); `components/public/OrderDetailChrome.tsx` (`Icon` + `ICON_TICK`/`ICON_CLOCK`/`ICON_ALERT`/`ICON_IMAGE`/`ICON_DOC`/`ICON_PRINT`/`ICON_DOWNLOAD`, `OrderCard` with `title`/`note`/`flush`/`wide`/`foot`, `OrderNote` with a required `info|ok|warn|bad` tone); `components/public/OrderProgressRail.tsx`; `components/public/OrderItemList.tsx`; `components/public/OrderDocuments.tsx` (exports the `OrderDocument` type the page builds its paperwork list from); and `lib/order-progress.ts` (`orderProgressSteps`, `orderStopped`) - pure, derived every render like the dispatch summary, never stored, and it marks everything before the first unfinished step done so the rail can never show a finished step after an unfinished one. `lib/order-display.ts` gains `formatOrderDateShort` ('3 Aug') for the rail. `components/public/OrderRequestPanel.tsx` swapped its own `.card`/`.card-title` chrome for `sod-card`/`sod-card-head`/`sod-card-body` so it matches the rest of the page (it is used on this page only). Three behaviour changes rather than pure restyling: **grouped lines now sit together** on this page (`sortLinesByGroup`/`effectiveGroup`, as the cart, checkout, confirmation and order emails already did - a desk and its drawer unit were the last two lines on the platform still printed unrelated); **the unit price shows where quantity is more than one** ('GBP 240.00 x 4' instead of leaving the reader to do the arithmetic); and **state and action stopped sharing a row** - 'Dispatched' and 'Buy again' were two pills of equal standing, now the badges sit left and the button right. The `.card` spacing bug went with it: globals' `.card` carries `margin-bottom: var(--space-6)` and every one of the old sections sat in a `gap: var(--space-4)` grid, so every gap on the page was the two stacked. Cancelled and refunded orders get a sentence instead of a rail (there is no next step to point at); `ON_HOLD` gets a callout above the rail. Colours are all tokens, light and dark; two physical breakpoints (620px pairs to one column, 480px drops the item price under the name), plus a `@media print` block and a stacked rail under 560px. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint .` clean, 909 shop tests green - the 5 skips are `lib/db/refund-netting.live.test.ts`, which is live-database gated and untouched by this. No schema, no SQL, no migration.)

Last updated: 2026-09-04 (**The invoice details panel stops offering a billing name** - `shop` v0.1.388. The panel added in v0.1.384 carried First name / Last name boxes; the checkout has never asked for a billing name (an invoice is addressed to the company, or to whoever placed the order), so it was offering a field with no question behind it. Off the form in `components/public/OrderBillingPanel.tsx`, and off the read-only view above it - a line nobody may change, over a form that does not offer it, only invites the question. **The route is the half that matters**: `AddressSchema` in `app/api/member/orders/[id]/billing/route.ts` now accepts `line1`, `line2`, `city`, `county`, `postcode` and NOTHING else, with `firstName`/`lastName`/`country`/`phone` read off `order.billingAddress ?? order.shippingAddress` server-side. A name is part of WHO is being billed, so a route taking one from the browser would have let a hand-rolled request change the party on an invoice while looking like an address correction. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, 5286 tests green on the merged tree (three other sessions' work - v0.1.385, v0.1.386, v0.1.387 - landed in this checkout between v0.1.384 and this). **Shared-tree note, second time today:** `FIELD_NOTES.md` and `wiki/Shop.md` were both found on disk MISSING another session's committed entries - a whole-file write from a stale copy - and were restored with `git checkout --` rather than committed, which would have silently reverted their work. Diff before you stage in a tree with other agents in it: a `git diff` that is pure deletions is not your change.)

Last updated: 2026-09-04 (**A refund taken before the order is invoiced is left OFF the invoice instead of credited** - `shop`, uncommitted working tree. A line goes out of stock while the order is being picked, the money goes back, and only later does the order reach whatever the shop invoices on: there is nothing to credit, because nothing was invoiced. Previously the invoice went out for the whole basket, so the customer got a VAT invoice for goods they never had and the shop declared output tax on money it had already returned - and the order screen offered **Raise the credit note** against an invoice that did not exist. **The invariant now is that every refund is dealt with exactly once: netted off an invoice, or credited off one, never both and never neither.** New pure `lib/invoice-net-of-refunds.ts` (`netOrderOfRefunds`) takes settled refund lines off an order before `buildInvoiceMoney` sees it - returns the SAME object identities when there are no refunds, so the overwhelming majority of invoices are byte-for-byte what they were. Per line it works on the CHARGED gross (`total * (1 - discountRatio)` plus tax where EXCLUSIVE - the discount ratio matters, because `prepareRefund`'s cap is on the UNdiscounted value and the money that actually went back is the discounted one), takes the refund off it, drops the line when nothing is left and scales `total`/`taxAmount` together otherwise; quantity can legitimately land at 0 where somebody held part of the money back on every unit, and that line stays because the money is still the shop's. Delivery and its tax survive untouched (`order.taxAmount` less the ORIGINAL goods tax), the discount scales by what is left of the goods, and the total is recomputed rather than subtracted. **New migration `037_refund_netted_off_invoice.sql`** (001_initial edited to match) adds `shp_refunds.netted_off_invoice_id` + index. It exists because DATES CANNOT ANSWER THIS: a refund can be PENDING when the invoice is raised and settle seconds later, so it is older than the invoice and yet was not taken off it, and it needs a credit note like any other. `lib/db/refunds.ts` gains `listUncreditedRefundLines(orderId, alsoNettedOffInvoiceId?)` (COMPLETED, no credit note row, not already netted - the second argument lets ONE invoice's own marks back in, which is exactly what the reissue path needs so a replacement comes out at the figures the original did), `markRefundsNettedOff` and `clearRefundsNettedOff`; `ShpRefund` gains `nettedOffInvoiceId`. `buildInvoiceInsertInput` now returns `{ input, nettedRefundIds }` (**breaking for any caller** - `issueInvoiceForOrder` and `lib/invoice-reissue.ts` both updated) and marks them once the invoice row exists; `voidInvoiceAndTellSinks` clears them, since the document that absorbed them is gone. `issueCreditNoteForRefund` refuses a netted refund in plain English, and its no-invoice refusal now reads as "nothing to credit" rather than an error. Admin credit-note GET carries `nettedRefundIds` and `invoiced`; `OrderDetailScreen` splits the uncredited list three ways - netted (a sentence, no button), settled-before-any-invoice (a sentence, no button), genuinely missing (the button, as before). 10 new tests in `lib/invoice-net-of-refunds.test.ts`. **The SQL is executed for real** by new `lib/db/refund-netting.live.test.ts` (5 tests, gated on `RUN_LEDGER_GUARDS`/`RUN_BACKUP_ROUNDTRIP`, throwaway `cactus_rt_shopnet_*` database on the OVH VPS, core init SQL applied first because shop's migrations reach for `Layout`) - added to the `test:ledger-guards` script. `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint` clean, 5293 tests green, `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a real PASS (4 tests, 108s).)

Last updated: 2026-09-04 (**The tracking link shows on the customer's own order page** - `shop` v0.1.386, released. Parcels have carried `tracking_url` since v0.1.382 and both dispatch emails send it, but `app/public/shop/account/orders/[id]/page.tsx` printed the tracking NUMBER and nothing else - the one page a customer goes back to once the email is buried made them work out whose website to paste it into. One `Track` anchor per parcel now, labelled `Track your parcel` for a single parcel and `Track parcel N` where there are several, `target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"`, `--color-primary`. **The http/https check moved out of `lib/order-status.ts` into new `lib/tracking-url.ts`** (`safeTrackingUrl`): two surfaces now render this value as an href and neither should take "the writer validated it" on trust - the dispatch route still refuses anything else on the way in, this is the same rule on the way out for rows written before that check existed, imported, or set by hand. 3 new tests. **Note for whoever reads the history:** ANOTHER SESSION was given this same task in parallel and wrote the release notes that now sit on tag v0.1.386, which points at this session's commit `d901093`; their own attempt is not in the tree. Two agents in `modules/shop` at once cost three version-number collisions today (0.1.380, 0.1.382 and this one) and one commit that had to be unpicked because it swept up the other session's uncommitted work - see `git reset --mixed` + `git hash-object`/`git update-index --cacheinfo` if it happens again, which stages a rebuilt blob without touching the shared working tree. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, 5286 tests green. Core pin: carried by v0.5.1516, which pins v0.1.387 and so includes this.)

Last updated: 2026-09-04 (**The whole-order dispatch email carries tracking LINKS, not just numbers** - `shop`, uncommitted working tree. `shp_shipments.tracking_url` and the partial-dispatch email's link landed in v0.1.382 (another session, same day); the whole-order path never saw it, because `dispatchDetails` only gathered number and carrier. It now returns a `DispatchDetails` with `trackingUrl` and `trackingLinks` as well. **The awkward bit is that one `{{trackingUrl}}` is one anchor and three parcels are three**, so both are offered: `trackingUrl` is filled ONLY when there is exactly one distinct link (the ordinary case, and the only one where a lone anchor cannot point at the wrong parcel), and `trackingLinks` is a block of one `<p><a>` per parcel labelled `Track <number>` - or `Track your parcel` where the parcel has a link but no number. Duplicate urls across parcels collapse to one. Flags `hasTrackingUrl` / `hasTrackingLinks` alongside. `shop.status-shipped`'s default body prints the block; `trackingLinks` is a **rawTag**, so both the href and the label go through `escapeHtml` here. New private `safeTrackingUrl` re-validates http/https with `new URL()` even though the dispatch route already does: the value becomes an `href` in front of somebody who trusts the sender, and a row written before that check existed - or by hand - has never been past it. 8 new tests in `lib/order-status.test.ts` (11 total; the four existing ones grew a `NONE` spread as the return type widened). `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, 5273 tests green.)

Last updated: 2026-09-04 (**Customers can correct who their invoice is made out to, and a company change credits and reissues it** - `shop`, uncommitted working tree. The distinction the whole change turns on is statutory: a moved ADDRESS is the same party being billed for the same supply, so the document is corrected in place; a changed COMPANY is a different legal person, so the invoice is credited in full and replaced. **New migration `036_invoice_billing_changes.sql`** (001_initial edited to match for fresh installs) adds `shp_invoices.customer_amendments` JSONB, `superseded_at`, `superseded_by_invoice_id` (self FK, ON DELETE SET NULL), `supersede_reason`, and **replaces the partial unique index** `shp_invoices_order_issued_key` with `shp_invoices_order_live_key` on `(order_id) WHERE status='ISSUED' AND superseded_at IS NULL` - the old one made a replacement impossible to insert. `insertInvoice` matches BOTH index names on 23505, since an install that has not taken 036 yet still reports the old one. The original stays **ISSUED, not VOID**: void says withdrawn-before-anybody-acted, and with a credit note beside it the books would reverse the same sale twice. New `lib/customer-billing.ts` holds every rule, pure and shared by page and route (`customerBillingEditOffered`, `customerCanEditBilling`, `billingChangeEffect` -> `none`/`order`/`reissue` + `amendsInvoice`, `reissueAvailable`, `reissueWarning`, `normaliseCompanyName`/`normaliseAddress` - case and spacing only, punctuation kept, so "Acme Ltd." is NOT "Acme Ltd"), 21 tests in `lib/customer-billing.test.ts`. New `lib/invoice-reissue.ts` is the one place a change happens: credit note + supersede + replacement invoice go in **one `prisma.$transaction`** (`supersedeInvoice` is guarded on the row still being live, so two simultaneous presses cannot both raise a replacement), sinks and email after the commit. `lib/db/invoices.ts` gains `amendInvoiceBillingAddress` (the ONE writer that edits a snapshot - address only, history captured inside the same UPDATE), `supersedeInvoice`, `linkSupersedingInvoice`, and `getInvoiceForOrder` now excludes superseded rows; `insertInvoice`/`insertCreditNote` take an optional `PrismaTransactionClient` (core's exported type - `Prisma.TransactionClient` does NOT match the extended client). `lib/invoices.ts` extracts `buildInvoiceInsertInput` and `siteTimezone`, exports `buildWording`, adds trigger `REISSUE`; `lib/credit-notes.ts` extracts `buildFullCreditNoteInput` (subtotal is BACKED OUT of the total - the credit note table has no discount column) and `creditWording`, exports `tellTheBooks`. New `lib/db/orders.ts` `setOrderBillingIdentity` (organisation + billing address in one statement; never touches the delivery address). New route `app/api/member/orders/[id]/billing/route.ts`, PATCH, **two-phase**: a company change on an issued invoice comes back `{needsConfirmation, warning}` and only a second request carrying `confirm` goes through. New client `components/public/OrderBillingPanel.tsx`; the account order page now lists EVERY issued invoice (superseded ones marked "(cancelled)") rather than only the live one. New config `customerBillingEditEnabled` and `customerBillingReissueEnabled`, both default false, with their section under Settings -> Shop -> Checkout. New email template `shop.invoice-reissued` / trigger `INVOICE_REISSUED` - one email for both documents, because a bare credit note reads as money coming back. **Both documents travel as attached PDFs and neither is linked in the body**: the credit note is what cancels the invoice the buyer's accounts department has already filed, so `emailTheCustomer` prints it alongside the replacement (`invoiceEmailAttachment` + `creditNoteEmailAttachment` in parallel, each still allowed to come back null) and the default wording gained `hasInvoicePdf` / `hasCreditNotePdf` conditionals. `invoiceUrl` and `creditNoteUrl` are still passed and still offered as tags, so an owner who has edited this email keeps their links working. **`orderCompanyName` changed, and it is the one behaviour change outside the new feature**: the DELIVERY address fallback now only applies to an order carrying no billing address of its own (SQL twin `ORDER_COMPANY_SQL` in `lib/db/orders.ts` matched, verified by running it against the live Deskwell schema read-only). Without it, a customer clearing their company had it handed straight back by the delivery label - and migration 027 backfilled `customer_organisation` out of that very field, so that is every older order. Admin order screen shows a **Replaced** badge and the reason for a superseded invoice, so two ISSUED rows are not left unexplained (`present()` in the admin invoice route carries the two new fields). `ShpInvoice`'s four new fields are **optional on purpose**: the type doubles as the shape a proforma, a credit note and quote-for-shop's own document are presented in, and none of those can be superseded. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, 5264 tests green, and `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a real PASS (4 tests, 117s) - though note its harness builds a subset of module schemas that does not include shop, so the shop columns are covered by `lib/backup/schema-coverage.test.ts` rather than by an actual round-trip. The DDL itself WAS executed for real: a throwaway `cactus_rt_*` database on the OVH VPS ran the 023-era table then 036 on top of it twice, the fresh-install shape from 001 then 036, and the amendment UPDATE, the supersede guard and the index still refusing two live invoices - temporary test file, since deleted, database dropped.)

Last updated: 2026-09-04 (**Order emails link each line to its exact product or variation** - `shop`, uncommitted working tree, same session as the entry below. `OrderEmailLine` gains `url`; `renderOrderItemsTable` wraps BOTH the name and the thumbnail in one `<a>` (new `linked()` helper, `color:#333333;text-decoration:underline` - no shop palette exists inside an email, and a hardcoded brand blue would clash with every wrapper that is not blue). New `productEmailUrl(slug, siteUrl, style)` exported from `lib/order-items-email.ts`: absolute via `productUrl`, null when either half is missing, because a relative href resolves to nothing in an inbox. **The variation case needs no special handling and must not get any**: a variant is a catalogue-hidden child product with its own slug, and shop's product page hands an unshowable slug to `shop.product-page-resolver`, which is where shop-variations opens the parent's page on that variation - so the line's OWN slug is already the exact-variation link, and this module never learns how variations are wired. New slim `getProductSlugsByIds` in `lib/db/products.ts` (id + slug only, not `getProductsByIds`' `SELECT *`: these emails are assembled inside a payment webhook). `orderEmailLines` and `renderOrderItemsEmailTable` widen their config Pick to `'currencySymbol' | 'productUrlStyle'`; slug and media reads run in one `Promise.all`, each `.catch`ed to an empty map so a failed read costs the links or the pictures and never the email. `lib/shipment-email.ts` does the same for its two lists and moves `getShopConfigCached()`/`getSiteUrl()` above the line building, since the link needs the URL style. Covers ORDER_CONFIRMED, PAYMENT_RECEIVED, ORDER_PLACED_UNPAID, both ADMIN_NEW_ORDER alerts, PARTIAL_SHIPPED and STATUS_* - every table goes through the one renderer. 6 new tests in `lib/order-items-email.test.ts` (19 total). `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint modules/shop` clean, 909 shop+email tests green.)

Last updated: 2026-09-04 (**Courier and tracking move entirely onto the parcel, and a parcel can carry a tracking link** - `shop`, uncommitted working tree, reversing part of the entry below from the same day. The Status card's **Courier** and **Tracking number** boxes are gone: two places asking for the same number is how they end up disagreeing, and the details describe a parcel rather than a status. `OrderDetailScreen` loses `dispatchCarrier`/`dispatchTracking` and the SHIPPED-only spread on `setStatus`, keeping a line of copy pointing at **Dispatch items**; the status route's `trackingNumber`/`carrier` body fields and `applyOrderStatusChange`'s two optional params go with them, so the implicit shipment recorded on SHIPPED is created with no courier details. `dispatchDetails` is untouched - the whole-order email still reads the numbers back off the recorded parcels. **New: `shp_shipments.tracking_url`** (`migrations/035_shipment_tracking_url.sql`, plus the column in `001_initial.sql`), wired through `ShpShipment`, `mapShipment`, `CreateShipmentInput` and `createShipment`'s INSERT. `DispatchModal` grows an optional **Tracking link** box; the dispatch route validates it with `new URL()` and accepts only `http:`/`https:`, because the value ends up as an `href` in front of a shopper and `javascript:` would otherwise be reachable. `lib/shipment-email.ts` passes `trackingUrl` + `hasTrackingUrl`, and `shop.partial-shipped` gains both merge tags and a `Track your parcel` anchor in its default body - core escapes the value, and an install whose owner has rewritten that body adds the tag themselves from the editor's chip list. SMS deliberately unchanged. `npm run typecheck` clean, `eslint modules/shop` clean, `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` a real PASS (4 tests, 117s) - though note `lib/backup/roundtrip.test.ts:136` skips any module whose SQL contains `$$`, which excludes `shop` wholesale, so the cover here is the `schema-coverage` backstop rather than a real shop round-trip.)

Last updated: 2026-09-04 (**Credit notes travel as a file, and a guard that catches merge tags nothing fills** - `shop`, uncommitted working tree, same session as the three entries below. `lib/invoice-attachment.ts` gains `creditNoteEmailAttachment(creditNoteNumber, config)` beside the invoice one - same dynamic imports, printed on `documentPageSetup('shopInvoice')` because a credit note is drawn on the invoice's layout, filename off `creditNotePdfFilenamePrefix`. New config `creditNoteAttachToEmail` (boolean, default true) with its checkbox under Credit notes, disabled unless the customer email AND PDFs are both on. `emailCustomer` in `lib/credit-notes.ts` takes the config, attaches the file, and passes `hasCreditNotePdf` / `hasCreditNoteLink`: the template prints "attached" when there is a file and the LINK ONLY when there is not, so a shop with PDFs off is not left sending neither. **Also fixed: `{{#if hasReason}}` on shop.credit-note-issued has existed since the template was written and NOTHING has ever passed `hasReason`** - the reason a refund was given had never once printed, because a conditional whose flag is missing drops silently and no check in the repo looked. New `lib/email-templates.test.ts` is that check: for every shop template, every `{{tag}}` and `{{#if flag}}` in its own default subject and body must appear in `mergeTags` (site tags excepted), every `rawTags` entry must be a declared merge tag, and every key must be `shop.`-prefixed. It found EIGHT more templates whose conditional flags were never offered in the editor - `hasCustomerReference`, `hasPreOrderItems`, `hasPaymentInstructions`, `hasPaymentReference`, `hasItems`, `hasAdminNote`, `hasRefund`, `hasCustomerNote` - all now declared. Declaring a flag changes nothing at send (mergeTags only drives the editor chips and preview samples, and a `[hasX]` sample is not 'true' so previews are unchanged); it means an owner rewriting the email can discover the block instead of silently losing it. **Watch the scan trap if you repeat it:** a block boundary taken at the next `key: 'shop.` swallows the COMMENT above the next template, so a comment quoting `{{trackingNumber}}` briefly added it to shop.status-processing's tags - removed. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, 5245 tests green across two consecutive full runs, 42 new.)

Last updated: 2026-09-04 (**Tracking numbers now reach the customer on a whole-order dispatch** - `shop`, uncommitted working tree, same session as the two entries below. Reported as "tracking is not added to their email". Verified against the live database before touching anything: `shp_shipments` is EMPTY and no PARTIAL_SHIPPED or STATUS_SHIPPED has ever been sent, so this is a code gap and not a data one, and `shop.status-shipped` carries a Deskwell override that asks for `{{orderItems}}`/`{{orderTotal}}`/`{{shippingAddress}}` - none of which the status path passed, and an unfilled merge tag collapses to nothing rather than complaining. Three defects: (1) `applyOrderStatusChange` passed exactly three vars (customerName, orderNumber, shopName) to every status email; (2) the implicit shipment it records on SHIPPED was created with `trackingNumber: null` and there was nowhere in the UI to type one for a whole-order dispatch; (3) `shop.partial-shipped`'s `mergeTags` omitted `hasTracking`/`hasCarrier`/`hasOutstanding`/`isFinalPart`, so the editor never offered the flags and a rewritten body would drop the tracking line silently. Fixes: `applyOrderStatusChange` takes optional `trackingNumber`/`carrier` (onto the implicit shipment) and now passes the full order vars - items table, total, address, customer reference - plus new exported `dispatchDetails(shipments)`, which joins the DISTINCT numbers off `getShipmentsForOrder` rather than echoing the argument, so an order dispatched in three parcels quotes all three. Gathered only on SHIPPED. `shop.status-shipped` gains a real default body (items, courier, tracking, address), `rawTags: ['orderItems']`, and the other three status templates get the same merge tags offered. Status route takes the two new optional fields; `OrderDetailScreen` grows Courier and Tracking boxes in the Status card, shown only while the order is not already dispatched, sent only when the chosen status is SHIPPED. Bulk status route deliberately unchanged - a bulk bar has no per-order tracking to give. 4 new tests in `lib/order-status.test.ts`. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, 5199 tests green.)

Last updated: 2026-09-04 (**Order emails list what was bought as a photographed table, not one run-on paragraph** - `shop`, uncommitted working tree, same session as the invoice attachment below. Reported as a five-item order arriving as a single unbroken sentence: the item list was a newline-joined plain string, and HTML collapses every one of those newlines to a space, so the text part read correctly and the HTML part - which is what everybody actually sees - did not. New `modules/shop/lib/order-items-email.ts`: `renderOrderItemsTable(lines)` (pure, table-based, every rule inline, no CSS custom properties - core's email constraints, see `lib/email/blocks.ts`), `orderEmailLines(items, config)` (bulk media read, at most one extra query per email), `renderOrderItemsEmailTable` for the two together, and `absoluteImageUrl(url, siteUrl)` - an inbox has no origin, so a site-relative `src` is a broken picture in every client; anything neither http(s) nor `/`-rooted is dropped. Columns earn their place: the picture column appears only when a line has one (blank `&nbsp;` cell for the pictureless lines, or names slide left), the price column only for a priced list. Wired into `lib/order-fulfillment.ts` (ORDER_CONFIRMED / PAYMENT_RECEIVED / ADMIN_NEW_ORDER), `lib/order-placed-email.ts` (ORDER_PLACED_UNPAID / ADMIN_NEW_ORDER_UNPAID) and `lib/shipment-email.ts` (PARTIAL_SHIPPED, which now reads `getOrderItems` purely to get a product id per `orderItemId` - the dispatch summary carries names but no product). **Two things had to change in `lib/email-templates.ts` together:** `rawTags` now names `orderItems` / `dispatchedItems` / `outstandingItems` (core escapes every non-raw tag, so the markup would have arrived as visible angle brackets), and `<p>{{orderItems}}</p>` is unwrapped to bare `{{orderItems}}` - a table may not sit inside a `<p>`. An install whose owner has EDITED one of those bodies keeps their `<p>` wrapper; parsers auto-close it, which is untidy rather than broken. Thumbnail is the LINE'S OWN product's primary image, i.e. the variation's child product - deliberately NOT the parent's, because the parent link lives in `svr_variants`, a shop-variations table this module must not read, and the thank-you page and account order page already resolve it exactly this way. 13 new tests in `lib/order-items-email.test.ts`. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, 5195 tests green.)

Last updated: 2026-09-04 (**The VAT invoice now travels with the "your order is complete" email** - `shop`, uncommitted working tree. New `modules/shop/lib/invoice-attachment.ts` exports `invoiceEmailAttachment(orderId, config)`, returning an `EmailAttachment` or `null`; new config key `invoiceAttachToEmail` (boolean, default true) sits beside `invoicePdfEnabled`/`invoicePdfFilenamePrefix` in `lib/config.ts`, with its checkbox under Invoices in `components/admin/ShopSettingsTab.tsx` (disabled while PDFs are off). `lib/order-status.ts` attaches it on `COMPLETED` only, and the lookup sits AFTER the `if (changed)` block so an order invoiced on that very transition sends the invoice it has just been given rather than the nothing it had a moment earlier. `getInvoiceForOrder` is ISSUED-only, so a voided invoice is never re-sent; null covers setting off, PDFs off, no invoice raised, and a printer that fell over, and none of them may stop the email. Both `invoice-pdf` and `invoice-document` are dynamic imports inside the function - the document module drags in Puck's RSC renderer and every invoice block, and a status change that is not a completion has no business loading it. Deliberately NOT on `STATUS_SHIPPED`: a shop invoicing on despatch would send the same document twice. Printed with `documentPageSetup('shopInvoice')`, so the file in the inbox is on the same sheet as the download. No DDL, so no backup gate. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` clean, 799 shop tests green.)

Last updated: 2026-09-04 (**A proforma order now says which half of the dance it is in, and the payment note can go to the supplier's accounts desk** - `purchase-orders` v0.1.30, uncommitted working tree, on top of the same version's document filing. **Status:** reported as "a proforma was added but it still says Sent", and it did. The switch is forced false without an address in both the form and `toSupplierInput`, so it can never send the note nowhere. 5 new tests in `proforma-stage.test.ts`. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint .` clean for this module, module suite green, backup round-trip 4/4 real PASS. **Two unrelated things seen in the shared tree and left alone:** `modules/filters-for-shop/lib/variation-links.ts` is deleted in another session's working tree, which is 20 `tsc` errors in `filters-for-shop`/`product-discovery-tool` and stops three `purchase-orders` test files COLLECTING through the generated extension-point graph (proved green with that one specifier stubbed: 92/92). Last updated: 2026-09-04 (**Supplier paperwork now files from BOTH ends, and reads its own reference number off the file** - `purchase-orders` v0.1.30, uncommitted working tree. Migration `009_payment_proof.sql` adds `po_orders.proforma_payment_proof_media_id` + `proforma_proof_sent_at` (001 carries both for fresh installs); `clearProformaPayment` now nulls the sent stamp too, and `lib/media-usage-provider.ts` gained a fourth UNION arm so the library never offers a proof of payment up as clutter. `POST .../proforma` takes `sendProof`, fetches the filed proof through core's `downloadMedia` (new `mediaAttachment` in `lib/proforma.ts`) and hangs it on the existing *Proforma paid* email - `sendProformaPaid` gained a `proof` parameter and adds its own "attached" sentence only when one actually travels; `proforma_proof_sent_at` is stamped only on a successful send. With `sendProof` the route no longer short-circuits on `alreadyPaid`, which is what makes **Send the proof again** work on an order paid last week. **New `lib/pdf-text.ts`: a PDF text reader written by hand** - a module cannot add an npm dependency, core's package.json is what an install installs - covering brute-force object scan (no xref parsing, so appended and compressed-xref files still read), FlateDecode or no filter, the standard security handler for the EMPTY user password (RC4 V1/V2 by hand, since OpenSSL 3 drops RC4 without the legacy provider, plus AESV2; R5/R6 refused rather than guessed), and text operators into lines. `lib/document-reference.ts` turns that into a guess: labelled search first (*Invoice No.*, *Sales No.*, value beside the label or on the next line), filename second. It refuses dates, money, camera/scanner names (`IMG_0042`) and - the trap that prompted the rule - **our own PO number, which is on their acknowledgement under *Cust Order No.***. Applied at both doors: the admin upload and `app/api/public/portal/upload/route.ts`, and in both only where the box was left empty AND the order carries no reference already. The two real supplier PDFs that prompted this were verified locally and are NOT in the repo (a real customer's address is on them); the committed tests build a PDF of the same shape instead - `pdf-text.test.ts` (6) and `document-reference.test.ts` (13). `ProformaCard` in `OrderScreen.tsx` rebuilt around four rows (proforma, proof of payment, paid, acknowledgement) with `FiledDocument`/`FilePicker`/`ReferenceField` helpers; `ReferenceField` is KEYED on the server value at both call sites rather than syncing with an effect, which `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` rightly refuses. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint .` clean, 5164 tests green, and the backup round-trip gate run for real against a throwaway OVH database: 4/4 PASS, no skip.)

Last updated: 2026-09-04 (**A filtered or discovered card now opens the product on the variation that answers EVERY choice, not the first one** - `filters-for-shop` v0.1.49 + `product-discovery-tool` v0.1.7, uncommitted working tree. Answer three questions and the product page opened on one of your answers and two variations' worth of somebody else's. `getProductFilterMatches` now keeps a representative variation per COMBINATION as well as per filter: `FltProductMatches.combos` is `Map<string, FltCombo[]>` (`{ filterIds, href }`) instead of `Map<string, string[][]>`, the fold query gaining a `DISTINCT ON (product_id, combo)` over a three-level derived table so the earliest variation in the owner's order wins and the outer `ORDER BY variant_position` puts a product's combinations back into that order. `internVariations` emits `links` alongside `byProduct` - `[shared prefix, one tail per entry]`, folded with `swap-pack`'s now-exported `commonPrefix`, entry-for-entry aligned with the ids (the duplicate-combination case keeps the FIRST variation's link, which is what makes the alignment true). New `chosen` memo unions them into a selection used by the dressing pass ONLY; `selected` stays the shopper's own ticks, because the questions, the counts and the address must never offer a locked filter as something to untick. **Cross-module ordering:** pdt's `requiresModules` floor for `filters-for-shop` goes to 0.1.49 - it imports `variation-links`, which does not exist in 0.1.48. Last updated: 2026-09-04 (**The floating narrow-down button was not exempt from core's button fill, so one colour setting gave two colours** - `product-discovery-tool`, uncommitted working tree. `.pdt-jump` is a lone `<button>` inside `.pdt-features`, which carries no `PDT_UNSTYLED` of its own (the marker sits on the module's chrome CONTAINERS, and this button has no container), so core's `main button:not([data-cactus-unstyled] …):hover{background:var(--btn-hover-bg) !important}` beat `--pdt-bar-btn-hover-bg` on the desktop twin while the phone's button - inside the marked `.pdt-bar` - honoured it. It also pins the other half of the rule: `.pdt-wrap` must NOT be marked, or the exemption (which covers a whole subtree with no way back in) would take the site's hover off every product card's buttons in the results. `tsc`, `eslint .`, 5115 tests green.)

Last updated: 2026-09-03 (**Custom Puck fields have to draw their own labels** - `product-discovery-tool`, uncommitted working tree. Puck renders no label for a `type: 'custom'` field - the render owns the whole row - so `flowField` and `layoutField` arrived in the sidebar as bare controls, the card-layout one reading only "Use shop default" at the bottom of the panel with nothing saying what it was. My first fetch had `age: 1831` and returned the pre-update sheet, which I read as "the site is running 0.1.1" - wrong, and the correction is the point: **on that host a plain GET can be half an hour stale, so cache-bust before concluding anything about what is deployed.** The remaining explanation for a sidebar with no colour fields is an editor tab held open across the deploy, serving the previous build's JS.)

Last updated: 2026-09-03 (**The question bar stops sticking; a floating button fetches it back, and the step heading stops drawing a box** - `product-discovery-tool`, uncommitted working tree. The across-the-top questions were `position:sticky` with an `.is-stuck` ground; both are gone, and with them the sentinel, its absolute-positioning rule, the `rootMargin` offset arithmetic and `StepFeatures`' `collapseSignal` (shutting the open question on scroll only existed because a pinned full-width one covered the products - nothing is pinned now, so a shopper who scrolls back finds it as they left it). In their place: an IntersectionObserver on the PANEL itself - `!isIntersecting && boundingClientRect.bottom <= 0` is exactly "the questions are off the top", with the bottom check ruling out the below-the-fold reading - and a `.pdt-jump` button, `position:fixed;top:var(--pdt-sticky-top,7rem);left:50%`, carrying `.pdt-bar-btn` as well so ONE set of block colour settings dresses the phone's bar button and this one. It calls `scrollIntoView`, and `scroll-margin-top:var(--pdt-sticky-top,7rem)` on the panel is what stops that landing under the site's fixed header. `.pdt-features.pdt-pos-top` stays `display:block` - it no longer has to be, but a two-row grid is not better and the reason is one edit away from mattering again. **Second fix, unrelated: `.pdt-step-title` draws no focus ring.** The heading takes focus on every step change so the step is announced; Safari hands a programmatically focused element `:focus-visible` after an ordinary mouse CLICK, which put a box round "Which sort of desks?" and left it there through every later step. The heading is not tabbable, so there is no keyboard journey to indicate and the announcement - the actual point of the focus move - is untouched. Also removed a reflex `@media (prefers-reduced-motion:reduce){.pdt-jump{transition:none}}`: the button has no transition, and a SECOND reduced-motion block broke the existing test, which finds the first one - a good failure, since the next rule added to the wrong block would have been silently unprotected. `discovery-css.test.ts` at 15 cases. `tsc`, `eslint .`, 5112 tests green.)

Last updated: 2026-09-03 (**The narrow-down button floats, and a block can colour it** - `product-discovery-tool` v0.1.3, core pin 0.5.1494. `.pdt-bar` loses `background:var(--color-bg)` and its top rule: painted, it showed as a strip of page colour above and below the button wherever the section behind it was another shade, which on the homepage's subtle-background section it always is. The button carries `box-shadow:0 6px 18px rgba(0,0,0,.18)` instead, which is what lifts it off the products it floats over. Four new block fields - `barButtonBg`, `barButtonText`, `barButtonHoverBg`, `barButtonHoverText` - each a `SiteColourField` from `@/lib/puck/fields/registry` (the proxy, as `purchase-orders` imports it), so they offer the site's own palette as `var(--color-N)` AND carry a dark-mode arm encoded as `light-dark(l, d)`: the site sets `color-scheme`, the browser resolves the arm, and nothing on the render side has to know which mode is on - one value covers both, which is why this is four fields and not eight. Delivered as CUSTOM PROPERTIES on `.pdt-bar` rather than inline declarations on the button (a hover cannot be written inline, and the sheet already owns every state), with the module's own colour as each rule's fallback so a block setting none is byte-identical to before. The border follows the fill wherever one is set - a solid button ringed in the page's border colour reads as a mistake, and a fifth question nobody wants to answer is not the fix. Hovered text falls back to the RESTING text before the theme's, so colouring the fill alone still leaves a readable label. Two cases added to `discovery-css.test.ts`, 16 in all. `tsc`, `eslint .` green.)

What is STORED is still the url, because that is what the storefront renders and what both media mechanisms key on; the picker only stops it being typed, which is what puts the picture in the library's folders and therefore inside `discoveryMediaUsageProvider` and `discoveryMediaReferenceRewriter`. `HINT` moved into admin/shared for the field note. **The reported 404 was not a rewriter failure:** traced on the live database - the Pedestals node had been rewritten `.jpeg` -> `.webp` by this module's own rewriter when the file was optimised, the new url returns 200, and the dead one Chris saw was the old address in a cached page. The real hazard the trace turned up is the FILE: `data/product_discovery_2026_09_03/deskwell-furniture-finder.json` still held the pre-optimise url for that node AND the original pictures for four more that had since been changed by hand in the admin, so a re-import would have resurrected one dead url and reverted four deliberate edits. All five copied back out of the live flow, and both READMEs now say that a saved flow file's pictures go stale the moment one is edited on the site - download fresh from the Guidance tab before re-applying. `tsc`, `eslint .` green.)

Last updated: 2026-09-03 (**A flow writes its own step headings** - `product-discovery-tool` v0.1.2, core pin v0.5.1493. "What are you looking for?", "Which sort of desks?" and "What matters to you?" were literals in the shell; they are the flow's words, not the module's, so `pdt_flows` gains `first_step_heading`, `later_step_heading`, `features_heading` - all TEXT, all nullable, **no SQL DEFAULT on purpose**: empty means "the wording the module ships", so improving that copy later reaches every site that has not overridden it, which a baked-in default never would. NEW numbered migration `002_step_headings.sql` (idempotent `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`) with the same three columns added to `001_initial.sql` for fresh installs - the overlap is harmless and the ledger runs 002 once. Resolution is a pure `stepHeading()` in `lib/flow.ts` beside `PDT_DEFAULT_HEADINGS` and `PDT_CHOICE_TOKEN`, 5 new cases in `flow.test.ts`: the later-step template substitutes `{choice}` with the label AS WRITTEN (the derived fallback lowercases it mid-sentence, but an owner's own sentence may start with it), a template naming no choice is used verbatim at every depth, and a whitespace-only override counts as none - an owner clearing the box leaves `''`, not null. Carried through `PdtFlow`, `FLOW_COLUMNS`, `rowToFlow`, `PdtFlowUpdate`/`updateFlow` (tri-state CASE WHEN, as its neighbours), the PUT route's zod, the Flow tab's settings form (three inputs, the defaults as placeholders so an owner sees what they are replacing, plus a new `HINT` style in admin/shared), and the import/export file so a flow still moves between sites whole. **Both live gates run, both real passes:** `npm run test:discovery-sql` 11/11 (raw SQL is executed by no standard gate, and this change edits `FLOW_COLUMNS`, `updateFlow` and the import upsert) and `npm run test:backup-roundtrip` 4/4 (module migration touching columns). Note the round-trip's own module set does not include this module, so the new columns' coverage comes from `lib/backup/schema-coverage.test.ts`, which parses every `modules/*/migrations/*.sql` and passed in the ordinary suite. `tsc`, `eslint .`, 5111 tests green.)

Last updated: 2026-09-03 (**The questions across the top become a sticky filter bar, and nothing hides behind "More options" any more** - `product-discovery-tool` v0.1.2, core pin v0.5.1493. `StepFeatures` drops the secondary fold entirely: every question renders, importance now decides ORDER alone (primary first), and the `showSecondary` state and `.pdt-secondary-toggle` rules go with it. In `startCollapsed` mode - which is the across-the-top layout on a wide screen, and only that - opening a question closes the one before it, because an open question now takes `grid-column:1/-1` and lays its options out in `repeat(auto-fit,minmax(min(100%,215px),1fr))`, so two open would be the wall of ticks the layout exists to avoid. The bar is `position:sticky;top:var(--pdt-sticky-top,7rem);z-index:5`, the variable named after filters' own `--flt-sticky-top` and defaulting to the same 7rem. **`.pdt-features.pdt-pos-top` had to stop being a one-column grid and become `display:block`** (gap -> margin): a grid item's containing block is its grid area and a sticky box cannot leave its containing block, so the bar sat in row one of a two-row grid pinned to its own height and stuck to nothing at all. Filters' sidebar works precisely because its panel's grid area is the full height of the results beside it. **Stuck is detected with a sentinel, not a scroll handler**: `position:sticky` fires no event, so a zero-height absolutely-positioned `.pdt-sticky-sentinel` sits at the top of `.pdt-features` (absolute because in the grid flow it would be a row of its own and the 20px gap would push the bar down for a marker with no height) and an IntersectionObserver watches it with `rootMargin: -<offset>px 0 0 0`, where the offset is read off the bar's own computed `top` - so "the sentinel has left" means "the bar has stuck" to the pixel, at whatever offset the site sets, instead of the header's height later. On sticking, `collapseSignal` increments and `StepFeatures` shuts everything (render-phase adjustment, not an effect: `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` forbids the effect form and an effect would paint the open question once more first) and `.is-stuck` gives the bar a ground, a shadow and a 78vh cap with its own scroll. Padding is deliberately identical stuck and not - a sticky box keeps its space in the flow, so resizing it on stick shunts the page under it. `questionsStuck` is DERIVED from `stuckLayout && sentinelPassed` rather than reset in the effect, for the same lint rule. Verified against the live homepage's section wrapper: core clips a Section's overflow only for radius, background image, overlay, shadow or grid-scan (`config.core.tsx` ~1212), and `section-finder` has none of them, so sticky is not trapped. `discovery-css.test.ts` at 13 cases. `tsc`, `eslint .`, 5105 tests green.)

Which one cannot be decided in CSS - `auto-fit` picks the column count from the container's width - so `useGridColumns` reads the used `grid-template-columns` back off the element under a `ResizeObserver` and counts the tracks; `live.length % columns !== 0` is the gap test, and before the first measurement it renders `is-below`, the arrangement that is right at any count. Layout effect, not passive: after paint the move is a visible jump. Two new block settings: `firstStepFoot` ('show'/'hide', defaulting to HIDE so a block saved before the field existed gets the plainer step) gates that foot on the FIRST browse step only - deeper steps always have it - because skipping step one of a catalogue-wide flow asks for the whole shop, while step one of a category-scoped flow is a real question; and `defaultSort`, the same `FLT_SORT_OPTIONS` list the dropdown offers with 'recommended' standing in for the empty value, defaulting to `best-selling`. Sort is applied SERVER-side exactly as `ShopFilterGrid.rsc` does it (`sortValueFromParam` then `sortProductIds` over `productIds`, and the resulting `orderedIds` feeds the cards drawn, the `orderIndex` used to intern shelf membership AND the shell's `serverOrder`, so there is one answer to what order these are in) - sorting only in the browser would rearrange the grid under the shopper on hydration. `DiscoveryShell` starts `sort` at `defaultSort` and writes the sort param only when it differs, spelling "Recommended" as `FLT_SORT_RECOMMENDED_PARAM`, again mirroring filters. `.pdt-choice` gains shop's own card hover value for value (`translateY(-4px)`, `0 8px 30px rgba(0,0,0,.10)`, `.25s ease`) plus a `prefers-reduced-motion` block that drops the transform; `.pdt-choice-pic` / `.pdt-choice-icon` go 4:3 -> 1:1. `discovery-css.test.ts` at 10 cases. Last updated: 2026-09-03 (**The guided flow stops borrowing the site's button chrome, and the block gains four settings** - `product-discovery-tool` v0.1.1, core pin v0.5.1489. **The amber hover was never this module's**: core's `lib/design/tokens.ts` paints `main button:not([data-cactus-unstyled] …):hover{background:var(--btn-hover-bg) !important}` and recolours the text only on elements carrying `data-cactus-hover-fg`, so every button the wizard draws - a browse card, an answer chip, a "More about X" link - flipped to the theme gold with its own text colour intact and unreadable. `!important` cannot be outranked, so the fix is not to match: new `PDT_UNSTYLED` in `lib/types.ts`, spread onto the ten chrome containers (`pdt-progress`, `pdt-chips`, `pdt-browse`, `pdt-browse-foot`, `pdt-empty`, `pdt-questions`, `pdt-toolbar`, `pdt-picks`, `pdt-recovery`, `pdt-cards-failed`, `pdt-pager`, `pdt-bar`, `pdt-dialog`). Having opted out there is no fallback, so the module now owns every hover: `--color-primary-subtle` tint plus a primary border on cards, chips and the bar button; `.pdt-link:hover` and `.pdt-clear:hover` change `color` to `--color-text` and paint NO background (black on a light theme, white on a dark one, no pill behind three words); `.pdt-question-head:hover` underlines. Across the top, questions arrive SHUT (`startCollapsed = position === 'top' && !isSheet`): the module's own promise is that the products are on screen before anything is answered, and an open row of six questions with a note under every option puts them below the fold. Three UNIQUE indexes are EXPRESSIONS - `(flow_id, COALESCE(parent_id, ''), slug)`, `(flow_id, COALESCE(node_id, ''), group_id)`, `(filter_id, COALESCE(node_id, ''))` - because a plain UNIQUE over a nullable column constrains nothing (every NULL is distinct), and the matching `ON CONFLICT` clauses have to spell the expression identically.

Public: Puck blocks `ProductDiscovery` (+`.rsc`), `DiscoveryHeader` (+`.rsc`), `ProductDiscoveryLauncher` (+`.rsc`); layout type `productDiscovery` with `productDiscoveryStarters` seeded `publishByDefault: true`; `publicRootSlug` claim `discoveryClaimsRootSlug` -> `app/root/[slug]/page.tsx`. ONE server pass resolves the flow's product set, matrix, per-node shelf membership (interned as indexes into `serverOrder`) and page one of cards; the whole wizard then runs client-side in `components/public/DiscoveryShell.tsx` with `lib/cards-action.tsx` fetching later pages by id. Pure logic in `lib/flow.ts`, `lib/resolve.ts`, `lib/questions.ts`, `lib/compare.ts`, `lib/recovery.ts`, all unit-tested (50 tests).

Admin: ONE tab, `shop.products-tabs` order 42, four sub-tabs (Flow/Questions/Guidance/Insights); settings under `shop.settings-sub-tabs`. `lib/import-export.ts` + `scripts/import-flow.mjs` do slug-referenced JSON in and out, validate-then-apply, whole flow or nothing. Extension points: `core.menu-entity-provider`, `core.media-reference-rewriters`, `core.media-usage-providers`. API under `/api/m/product-discovery-tool/admin/**` plus one unauthenticated `public/stats` beacon whose choice keys must name something in the flow's own configuration, so row growth is bounded by the config rather than by the caller.

**Defect found by running the SQL, which no other gate does:** `slugOwnedElsewhere` guarded optional tables with `to_regclass(...) IS NOT NULL AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM "gz_posts" ...)`. Postgres resolves every relation while PARSING, so the guard is not a guard - the whole statement fails with `relation does not exist` on a site without gazette, and every flow save with it. Now two passes: `to_regclass` over a list of NAMES, then one statement per surviving table. `filters-for-shop`'s `ensureUniqueCollectionSlug` has the same shape and the same latent problem; it has never bitten because its optional tables belong to modules it requires. New gate `npm run test:discovery-sql` (`RUN_DISCOVERY_SQL=1`, `modules/product-discovery-tool/lib/discovery-sql.live.test.ts`) provisions a throwaway `cactus_rt_*` database on the OVH VPS, applies core + shop + filters + this module's schema and executes every raw statement this module ships: 11/11 PASS. `tsc`, `eslint .`, 5069 tests and a real backup round-trip PASS all green.)

The chase dropdown is gone: `followUpOptions(sendAt, timezone)` mirrors `snoozeOptions` word for word, with the same day-and-time box, and the chosen MOMENT is stored as the length between it and the send (`followUpMinutesBetween`) so a message leaving late is chased late. Live SQL suite 18/18 with 023 applied, backup round-trip a real PASS.)

Last updated: 2026-09-03 (**Chase it up, and stand it down** - `unified-inbox` v0.1.41, core pin v0.5.1479. A scheduled message can carry a follow-up - once it has gone the conversation is snoozed until the chase is due and assigned to whoever WROTE the draft, not whoever sent it - and inbound mail from the person a scheduled message is addressed to stands that message down rather than letting it ask a question they have just answered. Migration 022, `lib/follow-up.ts`, `holdScheduledDraftsFor`. Live SQL suite 18/18 and the backup round-trip a real PASS.)

Last updated: 2026-09-03 (**Write it now, send it later** - `unified-inbox` v0.1.40, core pin v0.5.1478. A draft can carry a departure time and goes out on its own; the scheduled-send cron claims due rows with SKIP LOCKED and the send route's idempotency key is the draft id, so one message goes once. Previously: **A layout type added to an EXISTING module now seeds its layout - core **v0.5.1474**, `filters-for-shop` **0.1.47**.** Root cause of the supplier-pages complaint two entries down, and it is structural, not a supplier bug. `Module.layoutsSeededAt` is ONE stamp for a whole module, and `planModuleSeeds` only gives a stamped module another go when `types.every((t) => !typesWithRows.has(t))`. Deskwell's shop has 48 `shopCategory` rows, so that is false forever: **any layout type added to shop in a later update can never seed**. `shopSupplier` reached a live site with starters and no layout, the page fell back to the plain grid, and the filter panel - the whole point - was absent until the owner hand-built a layout.

Counting Layout rows cannot fix it. "No rows for this type" reads identically whether the type is new or whether the owner **deleted** its layout on purpose to fall back to the built-in page, and re-minting the latter is core redesigning a live site unasked - which is exactly what the existing narrow test was protecting. So what has actually been seeded is now recorded.

**New `Module.seededLayoutTypes TEXT[] NOT NULL DEFAULT ARRAY[]::TEXT[]`** - schema.prisma, the init migration edited in place, and `prisma/core-reconcile/033_module_seeded_layout_types.sql` for existing installs. **Back-filled EMPTY on purpose.** New pure `planTypeSeeds(moduleName, ledger, declaredTypes)` in `lib/setup/starterLayouts.ts` returns `{seed, ledger}` with three rules: an EMPTY ledger on a stamped module means it predates the column, so it **adopts everything it declares today and seeds nothing** (no live site gets a layout minted retrospectively - only types appearing after this lands are ever seeded); a real ledger seeds whatever is declared but missing from it; and **declaring nothing means the module's code is absent from THIS build** (see isModuleInBuild), so the ledger is left exactly as it is rather than adopted empty, which would wipe it and let the next build re-seed every type.

`seedPendingModuleLayouts` gains a top-up pass before the existing full-seed loop, skipping any module that loop will handle. **It deliberately does NOT call `autoPlaceModuleBlocks`** - placement is first-install-only and re-running it would re-add marker blocks the owner has since deleted. A new layout type is not a new install. `seedModuleDefaultLayouts` takes an optional `onlyTypes`, narrowed BEFORE `planModuleSeedTemplates` so its one-per-type choice is made over the new types alone. The full-seed path now writes the ledger alongside the stamp.

**filters-for-shop 0.1.47** marks `starter-shop-supplier-filtered` `publishByDefault: true`, so the seeded supplier page is the one WITH the panel; flagged beats unflagged in `planModuleSeedTemplates`, and a shop without this module still seeds shop's plain starter. Note `moduleStarterTemplates(name)` filters by the type's OWNING module, so a contributed starter for `shopSupplier` is included when shop seeds - which is what makes this work at all.

Verified: `tsc` clean, `eslint` clean, `npm test` 4961 passed (6 new `planTypeSeeds` cases, including "keeps a type on the ledger after the owner deletes its layout"), **`npm run test:backup-roundtrip` real PASS** - mandatory here, the core init migration gained a column, and unlike the shop tables the round-trip DOES cover core's `Module`, so the new `text[]` is genuinely exercised against the `udt_name` rules. filters gate green on the tag before release.

**Standing rule now in memory**: a new Puck layout type ships with a default layout auto-created. A hardcoded fallback page is not an acceptable resting state when it cannot do what the feature was asked for.)

Last updated: 2026-09-03 (**A reply now puts an inbox conversation back in Open - snoozed or done** - `unified-inbox` v0.1.36, core pin v0.5.1473. v0.1.35 did the snoozed half. Previously: **The timezone sweep, pinned and finished - core **v0.5.1471** pinning `shop` **v0.1.376**, `unified-inbox` **v0.1.34**, `contact-form` **v0.1.40**, `contact-form-reply-catcher` **v0.1.13** and `advanced-shipping-for-shop` **v0.1.50**.** All five gates green on their tags, all five released. Sequence, for the record, because it could not be done in one pass: core v0.5.1470 shipped the `timezone.ts` / `timezone.server.ts` split with the four pins wound back to their last building versions, the modules were then tagged and gated against it, and this release re-pins them.

**Dead tags, left in place deliberately:** `shop` v0.1.375, `unified-inbox` v0.1.33, `contact-form` v0.1.39 and `contact-form-reply-catcher` v0.1.12 exist as git tags but have **no GitHub release** - their gates failed (or, for the last two, their code was superseded before release). Nothing pins them and the updater only ever offers releases, so they are inert. Do not create releases for them.

**Core v0.5.1469 is likewise superseded** - it pins two tags that do not build. It is no longer the newest release, so it is not offered.)
